# PART II: ON CHURCH SERVICES IN PARTICULAR (Canonical Hours, Vigil, and Divine Liturgy)

## Part II

### Concerning Church Services in Particular

Since public services, as was said above, are divided into three parts: (α) into pre-liturgical services, (β) into liturgical services, and (γ) into services following the Divine Liturgy; therefore, conformably unto this division, we divide this Part II into three sections: and in Section I we will speak in particular concerning pre-liturgical services; in Section II concerning liturgical services; and in Section III concerning the celebration of the Holy Mysteries and other sacred actions.

### Section I

§. 24.

Concerning Pre-Liturgical Services

The pre-liturgical services are the following: (α) Vespers;[^72] (β) Compline; (γ) Midnight Office; (δ) Matins; (ε) The First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours;[^73] and (ϛ) Typika.[^74] The aggregation of all these services is called the “Church Rule” (*pensum canonicum*). According to present practice, each service among the adduced divine services is not celebrated separately, but they are rather combined, so that the entire church rule is celebrated at three intervals; and in that manner each day hath a threefold service: evening, morning, and daytime. (α) The evening service consisteth of the Ninth Hour, Vespers, and Compline. (β) The morning service consisteth of the Midnight Office, Matins, and the First Hour. (γ) The daytime service consisteth of the Third and Sixth Hours, the Divine Liturgy, or the Typika. In this section we will speak first concerning the individual parts of the church rule, and secondly concerning the church rule according to diverse occasions.

#### Chapter I
Parts of the Church Rule

##### Article I

§. 25.

Concerning Vespers

Vespers according to their compositions are divided in general: (1) into ordinary and (2) extraordinary. (1) Unto ordinary Vespers belong: (α) Little Vespers; (β) Daily Vespers;

and (γ) Great Vespers, which endeth either without Litiya or with Litiya. Of extraordinary Vespers, however, we adduce only the most renowned, and these are: (α) Vespers for the departed; (β) Vespers in the Great Fast; (γ) Vespers with Liturgy; (δ) Vespers of Holy and Great Friday with procession; (ε) Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great and the blessing of loaves on Holy and Great Saturday; (ϛ) Vespers of Bright Week; (η) Vespers at the Descent of the Holy Spirit; (ϑ) Order of Vespers on the Eve of the Nativity, if this falleth on Saturday or on Sunday.

##### 1) Ordinary Vespers

§. 26.

Order of Little Vespers

The Priest, having come into the church and taken upon himself the epitrachelion, bowing before the royal doors maketh the usual beginning, that is: “Blessed is our God,” “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Most Holy Trinity,” “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice). (Thus far the usual beginning). And immediately the reader in a quiet voice readeth the evening psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” After the psalm: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice) without chanting. There is no Litany of Peace, but the Priest entereth into the sanctuary, and the choir instead of it saith: “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and immediately chanteth: “Lord, I have cried.” Stichera on 4, that is, from the verse: “From the morning watch.” At the conclusion of the stichera: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. After this the Priest at his place in the sanctuary proclaimeth: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” and the choir singeth: “Gladsome Light.” At “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” the choir singeth the Daily Prokimenon, after which the reader readeth: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” After “Vouchsafe” there is no litany “Let us complete our evening prayer,” but instead of this the choir saith: “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and immediately singeth the aposticha stichera (3). After “Glory,” and “Now and” followeth “Now lettest Thou,” then: Trisagion, “Our Father” with the Exclamation, Troparion of the feast or troparion unto the Saint, “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. After these the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary and chanteth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” with the Least Dismissal: from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.”

Little Vespers is celebrated on Feasts of the Lord (except the Nativity and Theophany), Feasts of the Mother of God, and of Solemn Saints always in the evening, namely, when on the morrow Great Vespers with Litiya and the blessing of loaves taketh place.

§. 27.

Order of Daily Vespers without Entrance

The Priest cometh forth in the epitrachelion before the royal doors, and having made bows, maketh the usual beginning as at Little Vespers, that is: “Blessed is our God,” “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Most Holy Trinity,” “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this, Psalm 103: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Upon the conclusion of this, “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God” with singing; and immediately the Priest loudly proclaimeth the Litany of Peace. After this litany the Priest goeth into the sanctuary, and the chanter readeth the kathisma of the day with three antiphons, but before it: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” Upon the conclusion of the kathisma: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen.” And immediately from Psalm 140 the first verse “Lord, I have cried” and the second verse “Let my prayer be set forth” are sung, while the remaining verses of the psalm are read. Psalm 141, “With my voice unto the Lord,” is also read, likewise also Psalm 129, “Out of the depths,” even unto the verse “If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities”; for here we set 6 verses and chant them unto the stichera appointed at “Lord, I have cried.” After “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest loudly proclaimeth: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” and the choir singeth: “Gladsome Light.” After this the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” whereunto the choir singeth the Daily Prokimenon which is in the Horologion, and afterwards there is read: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” Thereafter the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary before the royal doors and loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Let us complete.” Having finished this litany and with the second exclamation: “Blessed and glorified be the majesty,” he goeth into the holy sanctuary. After that, the aposticha stichera (3), then “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation. Troparion unto the Saint who falleth on the morning of the day. “Glory,” and “Now and,” Dismissal Theotokion according to the tone and day. During the singing of the troparia the Priest cometh forth, and after the singing saith the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and the Exclamation. After this the Small Dismissal from “Wisdom.”

This Vespers is celebrated on all weekdays, if on the morning of the day there be no Great Saint.

§. 28.

Order of Great Vespers with Entrance

(α) Great Vespers without Litiya. — The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion and bowed before the royal doors unto the holy sanctuary, pro- claimeth: “Blessed is our God,” and the choir saith: “Amen.” And immediately: “Heavenly King,” Trisagion, “Most Holy Trinity,” “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this usual beginning the reader or chanter readeth the “Introductory Psalm” [Psalm 103]: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” During this the Priest, standing before the royal doors, reciteth the Lucernarium prayers. When the psalm hath been read, the chanter saith: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice, with singing). The Priest loudly proclaimeth the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” After the Exclamation the Priest goeth into the sanctuary, and the chanter readeth the kathisma of the Psalter with antiphons, and that in such wise that before it he saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”; and after it he saith: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen.” After this from “Lord, I have cried” in the appointed tone the first two verses are sung. Having read the remaining verses of Psalms 140 and 141, the chanter setteth 10 verses (“Bring my soul out of prison,” etc.) or 8 (“Out of the depths”), and singeth the stichera appointed at “Lord, I have cried.” When the verse “For His mercy is confirmed” is said, then the Priest vesteth in the phelonion. When they begin to sing: “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest putteth incense before the Holy Table into the censer, and going forth around the holy table, by the northern side maketh the “entrance” with the censer, saying the prayer: “O good and man-befriending King, Who hast blessed all things: we diligently pray Thee, with contrite heart and humble spirit, bless our entrances and exits, O Christ our true God, for Thy coming, and ascension, and life with men is blessed, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.”[^75] Having taken his stand before the royal doors, the Priest blesseth the censer and censeth the holy sanctuary, the icon of Christ and of the Mother of God, and awaiteth the conclusion of the Theotokion. After this he openeth the royal doors, raiseth the censer, and traceth with it the sign of the cross, proclaiming: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” and the choir singeth: “Gladsome Light.” Afterwards the Priest, having entered into the holy sanctuary, censeth cruciformly around the holy table, and standing in the royal doors, turned toward the people, censeth the icon of Christ, of the Mother of God, and thus the people. After this the Priest goeth by the right side unto the high place. When the Priest is already seated upon the high place, upon the conclusion of the singing of “Gladsome Light,” the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” Unto this the choir singeth either the Great Prokimenon (4 times), and then there are no paremias; or the Little Prokimenon of the day (twice), and then after the prokimenon on Polyeleos feasts there occur 3 readings;[^76] and that in such wise that the Priest before each reading proclaimeth: “Wisdom!” and the reader: “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” or “The reading from the Proverbs,” or “The reading from the Catholic Epistle of Peter”; and again the Priest: “Let us attend!” and the reader readeth the paremia. During the reading of the 3rd paremia the Priest goeth by the left side unto the holy table. When the reader hath finished the paremia, the Priest shutteth the royal doors, and the litany “Let us all say” is sung. After this there is read “Vouchsafe,” and immediately the litany “Let us complete” is sung, and the Exclamation from the Priest: “For a good God and lover of mankind Thou art,” etc., “Peace be to all,” and again the Exclamation: “Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy kingdom.” Then they begin to sing the aposticha stichera (3), “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. After this “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation from the Priest. After the Exclamation the choir singeth the troparia according to custom, as the Typikon prescribeth. Upon the conclusion of the troparia the Great Dismissal taketh place, in which at “Wisdom” the royal doors are opened, and after the blessing they are shut. This Vespers is celebrated among us on Saturday evening and on other middle feasts on which at Matins there is a Polyeleos.

(β) Order of Great Vespers with Litiya. — The Priest, having entered into the church and made the usual bows before the royal doors, entereth into the holy sanctuary, and here putteth on the epitrachelion, having kissed the cross upon it, and vesteth in the phelonion. And vested he cometh unto the Holy Table, where, bowing low, he openeth the royal doors. Thereafter he blesseth incense with the prayer: “Incense we offer unto Thee, O Christ our God, for an odor of spiritual sweetness; which do Thou receive upon Thy heavenly altar, and send down upon us the divine grace and the gift of Thy Most Holy Spirit” — and censeth around the holy table, cruciformly, and the prothesis. Afterwards he cometh forth through the royal doors onto the ambo, where he censeth the icons and the entire church. He returneth again unto the royal doors, censeth the Holy Table, and unto the choir saying: “Command!” he proclaimeth: “O Lord, bless!” “Glory to the holy, consubstantial...” etc. After this Exclamation he entereth through the royal doors into the holy sanctuary, handeth over the censer, shutteth the royal doors, and having divested of the phelonion, in the epitrachelion cometh forth before the royal doors, where, having bowed, he reciteth the evening prayers. The choir during this time, beginning from “Come, let us worship,” readeth the Introductory Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” When the psalm is finished and the choir hath sung: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God” — the Priest before the royal doors loudly proclaimeth the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” After the Exclamation “For unto Thee is due,” the choir readeth the kathisma, saying beforehand: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”; while the Priest departeth unto his place in the sanctuary, and after the kathisma hath been read, secretly reciteth the litany: “Again and again” with the Exclamation “For Thine is the majesty,” and the choir singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen.” After this the choir singeth: “Lord, I have cried” and the proper stichera on 8 or on 10, and that in the occurring tone. When the choir singeth “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest, having vested in the phelonion and bowed unto the holy table, maketh the entrance with the censer, reciting within himself the entrance prayer: “O good and man-befriending King...” etc., and having come through the northern doors before the royal doors, boweth unto the holy table, and blessing the incense with three fingers, saith: “Blessed is the entrance of Thy holy ones, O Lord...” etc., and censeth the holy table, the icon of the Savior and of the Mother of God. Upon the conclusion of the sticheron he openeth the royal doors, maketh the sign of the cross with the censer, and proclaimeth: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” Thereafter, having entered into the holy sanctuary, at the steps he boweth unto the holy table; and while the choir singeth “Gladsome Light,” the Priest censeth around the holy table, and from the royal doors the icon of the Savior, of the Mother of God, and the people. After this the Priest handeth over the censer and departeth unto the high place. When the choir hath finished singing “Gladsome Light,” the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” Whereunto the prokimenon is sung. After the prokimenon the Priest proclaimeth: “Wisdom!” whereupon the reader beginneth the preface of the paremia, for example: “The reading from Genesis,” or “The reading from Exodus”; and the Priest again proclaimeth: “Let us attend!” and the reader readeth the paremia. Upon the conclusion of the readings, the Priest goeth up by the northern side unto the holy table, but first shutteth the royal doors, and here loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Let us all say...” etc., with the Exclamation: “For a merciful God...” etc. After this litany the choir readeth: “Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening,” and afterwards the Priest reciteth the second litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer...” etc., with the Exclamation: “For a good God...” etc. Further: “Peace be to all,” “Bow your heads unto the Lord,” and the Exclamation: “Blessed and glorified be the majesty...” etc. After this the choir cometh forth into the midst of the church and singeth the Litiya stichera (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Priest with the censer cometh forth around the holy table out of the sanctuary by the northern doors before the royal doors, saying nothing, and censeth the holy table, the icon of the Savior and of the Mother of God. After this, having handed over the censer, he goeth, with candle-bearers preceding, unto the prepared tetrapod in the midst of the church, whereupon 4 vessels are set cruciformly. — The dish with 5 loaves standeth on the side toward the sanctuary (lit. 'from the mountain'), and below (toward the west) standeth the vessel with wheat; the wine standeth on the side where the icon of the Most Holy Theotokos is, and the oil on the side where the icon of Jesus Christ is. And there he awaiteth the end of the singing. When the singing is concluded, he loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Save, O God...” etc.,[^77] with the Exclamation: “Hear us, O God.” After this litany followeth: “Peace be to all,” “Bow your heads,” etc., and the prayer which the Priest loudly reciteth: “O most merciful Master...” etc. And immediately the choir singeth the aposticha stichera (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and,” after which “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation. Upon the conclusion of this, first the Priest himself singeth the proper troparion, and the choir thereafter repeateth it, and that in such wise: if it be a Feast of the Lord or of the Mother of God, then the troparion of the feast is sung thrice; if, however, it be the feast of a Saint, then his troparion is sung twice, and “O Theotokos Virgin, rejoice” once. During the singing of the troparia the Priest cruciformly censeth the tetrapod whereupon the aforesaid offerings are prepared for blessing. Upon the conclusion of the singing the Priest loudly proclaimeth: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and readeth the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves in the wilderness and didst satisfy five thousand men: do Thou Thyself now, O Master, bless these loaves, wheat, wine, and oil,” with a single blessing[^78] he blesseth the loaves, wheat, wine, and oil. Upon the conclusion of this prayer, when the choir hath sung “Amen,” the Priest censeth the set-forth gifts and singeth: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” once; and the choir repeateth the same twice. After this the Priest departeth before the royal doors, and the choir readeth the psalm: “I will bless the Lord at all times” even unto “Shall not want any good thing” [Ps. 33:1–11], and addeth: “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father!” whereunto the Priest answereth: “Glory to the holy...” etc., and Matins followeth.

This Vespers among us is celebrated on great feasts in the morning, if Little Vespers hath previously taken place.

##### 2) Extraordinary Vespers

§. 29.

Order of Vespers for the Departed

This Vespers is daily without entrance. It is celebrated thus: The Priest in a black epitrachelion before the royal doors having bowed, maketh the usual beginning (“Blessed is our God,” etc.). After “Come, let us worship” the choir readeth the Introductory Psalm, while the Priest reciteth the evening prayers. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest chanteth the litany for the departed: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord”[^79] with the Exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection...” etc. And immediately the kathisma, after which “Lord, I have cried” is sung. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” on 6, namely: 3 martyria from the Oktoechos, and 3 for the departed. “Glory,” for the departed; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Oktoechos according to the tone. There is no entrance, but the Priest from his place proclaimeth: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” and the choir singeth: “Gladsome Light.” After this the Priest loudly proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir instead of a prokimenon singeth: “Alleluia” (4 times) in Tone 8 with verses for the departed,[^80] and immediately readeth: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” During this time the Priest cometh unto the royal doors, and here loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer...” etc., as at other times without any change. After this the aposticha stichera are sung, for the departed (3) with “Glory,” and “Now and,” and immediately “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation: “For Thine is the kingdom...” etc. After the Exclamation the troparia for the departed are sung (“With depth of wisdom,” “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Thee we have as a rampart”). During the singing the Priest cometh forth, and upon the conclusion of the singing loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,”[^81] (as at a Panakhyda) with the Exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection...” etc. Having finished the litany, he maketh the Small Dismissal. Namely: “Wisdom”; the choir: “More honorable,” etc. At the end, however: “May He Who hath dominion over the quick and the dead...” etc. Blessing, and “Blessed repose and eternal memory.”

This Vespers is celebrated on Friday evening on the so-called Memorial Saturdays.[^82]

§. 30.

Order of Vespers in the Holy Great Fast

(α) On Sunday[^83] evening such a Vespers is celebrated which up to the prokimenon: “Turn not away Thy face”[^84] or “Thou hast given an inheritance,” is great with an entrance; from here, however, that is, from “Vouchsafe,” it is little without the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” instead of which there is “Lord, have mercy” (40 times) with prostrations and with the least dismissal. The order of this Vespers is the following: The usual beginning: “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Come, let us worship,” Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” There is no kathisma on Sunday evening, unless on the following Monday there be a great feast. After this, the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” on 10, namely: 4 penitential from the Oktoechos,[^85] 3 from the Lenten Triodion,[^86] and 3 from the Menaion unto the Saint. “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. During the Theotokion the Priest vesteth in the phelonion and maketh the entrance with the censer. Great Prokimenon: “Turn not away,” or: “Thou hast given an inheritance” (4 times). After the prokimenon the Priest shutteth the royal doors, divesteth of the phelonion, and only in the epitrachelion, having taken his stand before the royal doors, upon the completion of “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” chanteth the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer.” And immediately in due order follow the aposticha stichera: 3 from the Lenten Triodion. “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion likewise from the Triodion.[^87] “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation. After the Exclamation the cantors and the rest of the people go forth into the midst of the church, and the troparion “O Theotokos Virgin” is sung with a prostration; at “Glory”: “O Baptist of Christ” with a prostration; at “Now and”: “Intercede for us” with a prostration. After this the Priest readeth: “Beneath Thy tender mercy” without a prostration. After this the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “O Lord, bless.” The Priest: “Blessed and glorified is Christ our God.” The choir: “Amen.” The Priest readeth: “Heavenly King,” etc.; and the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father.” The Priest: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master of my life,”[^88] etc., and immediately the least dismissal from “Glory to Thee.”[^89]

(β) If in the Great Fast there falleth on a fasting day (that is, from Monday unto Friday) a certain feast, either decretal, or church, or polyeleos, and if there be no Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, then Vespers from the beginning even unto the prokimenon is great with an entrance, but from here, that is, from “Vouchsafe” even unto the very end, it is little, but with the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and with the small dismissal. — The order of this Vespers is the following: The usual beginning (“Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” 12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried”: 3 of the Fast, and 5 unto the Saint; “Glory,” unto the Saint (if there be one); if not: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. Entrance. The prokimenon of the Fast is taken just as at other times. Readings: 2 of the Fast and 3 of the feast. After this the Priest putteth off the phelonion and in the epitrachelion cometh forth before the royal doors, during which time the cantor or reader readeth “Vouchsafe,” and here he loudly proclaimeth the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayers,” etc. Aposticha stichera of the feast: 3; “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion as usual. “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation. Troparia of the feast (“O Theotokos Virgin,” “O Baptist of Christ,” and “Intercede” are omitted). Upon the conclusion of the festal troparia the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors and chanteth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” After this, 3 prostrations, but without the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master.” Festal dismissal, and that the small one, from “Wisdom.”

(γ) Daily Vespers on fasting days of the Holy Fast (except Wednesday and Friday) is joined with the Ninth Hour and with the Typika. The Ninth Hour is celebrated thus: The Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary in the epitrachelion before the royal doors, saith: “Blessed is our God,” whereunto the choir answereth: “Amen,” and immediately: “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, “Our Father” with the Exclamation, “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Priest entereth into the sanctuary, and the cantor readeth the psalms, which are also found in the Molitvoslov.[^90] “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Kathisma of the day.[^91] After the kathisma, the troparion: “He Who at the ninth hour” with the verses, and “Deliver us not up” is read unto the end. After this, the Trisagion; “Our Father” with the Exclamation. Troparia: “Seeing the thief,” “In the midst of two thieves,” “The Lamb and Shepherd and Savior of the world,” which are only read. Upon the conclusion of these, “Lord, have mercy” (40 times); and the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary before the royal doors, and readeth the prayer: “Thou Who at every time.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be bountiful unto us,” and maketh 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the Typika beginneth in such wise that the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary, and the choir singeth: “In Thy kingdom.” Upon the conclusion of this it singeth: “Remember us, O Lord,” etc., “Remember us, O Master,” etc., “Remember us, O Holy One,” etc., and the people make prostrations at each one. After this the choir readeth: “The heavenly choir,” etc., “The choir of Saints,” etc., the Creed, “Remit, forgive,” “Our Father.” After this, the kontakion of the temple, of the day, and of the Saint. “Glory,” “With the Saints”; “Now and,” “Steadfast Protectress.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable.” After this the choir: “In the name of the Lord, bless, Father,” whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” and maketh 3 prostrations. After the prostrations: the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” (12 times) with the prayer: “O Master Christ God,” etc. Daily dismissal, in which also the Saint whose memory is observed is commemorated. After this beginneth Vespers, which is daily without entrance and without the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” but with 2 paremias. — It is celebrated thus: The Priest after the Typika proclaimeth: “Blessed is our God,” and the choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this, the psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” on 6 (3 from the Triodion and 3 unto the Saint). There is no entrance. There are two prokimena before the two paremias, but only before the 1st prokimenon doth the Priest proclaim: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” upon the conclusion of which “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” and the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Aposticha stichera of the Fast, and immediately: “Now lettest Thou.” Trisagion. “Our Father” with the Exclamation. After this the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors, and the choir into the midst of the church, and they sing the troparia with prostrations: “O Theotokos Virgin,” “O Baptist of Christ,” “Intercede for us, holy Apostles.” Upon the conclusion of these troparia the Priest proclaimeth: “Beneath Thy tender mercy.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). — And the Priest proclaimeth: “Blessed is He Who is,” etc., and readeth the prayer: “Heavenly King.” Further: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and the cantor concludeth: “In the name of the Lord,” etc., whereunto the Priest saith: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the choir on ordinary days beginneth anew: “Trisagion. Our Father with the Exclamation. Lord, have mercy (12 times)”; and the Priest readeth the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity” etc. (from the Typika). Upon the conclusion of this prayer the choir singeth: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (thrice) with prostrations, and after this readeth the psalm: “I will bless the Lord” [Ps. 33].[^92] And immediately the choir saith: “It is truly meet... unto... our God”; and the Priest loudly proclaimeth: “Wisdom,” etc. After this the Priest: “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc.; the choir: “Glory to the Father,” etc., and the daily dismissal with blessing.

(δ) On Wednesday and Friday of the Great Fast, Vespers is celebrated not only with the Ninth Hour and with the Typika, but also with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.[^93] The Ninth Hour and the Typika precede Vespers, while the Liturgy followeth Vespers. The Ninth Hour with the Typika is celebrated as was said above; only this is needful to note here, that when at the Typika the choir beginneth to sing “In Thy kingdom,” the Priest then entereth into the holy sanctuary and here vesteth in all the priestly vestments, saying over each vestment: “Let us pray unto the Lord.” After this he taketh his stand within the sanctuary before the Holy Table below, and here concludeth the Typika with the dismissal as was said above. — Upon the conclusion of the Typika the Priest approacheth the Holy Table, beginneth to pray: “Blessed is our God,” and having kissed the holy table and the cross, loudly proclaimeth: “Blessed is the kingdom,” whereunto the choir answereth: “Amen,” and “Come, let us worship” (thrice), etc. And immediately the litany of peace. After the Exclamation the 18th kathisma beginneth to be read in the midst of the church. Before the 1st Antiphon the cantor proclaimeth: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory to the Father...”; and the reader saith: “Both now and,” and readeth the 1st Antiphon. After the reading of the 1st Antiphon the Priest proclaimeth: “Again and again in peace let us pray unto the Lord,” and the cantor singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and the reader maketh 3 prostrations in the midst of the church. Upon the conclusion of the 2nd Antiphon the Priest again proclaimeth: “Again and again,” etc., and the cantor again singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord. Amen”; and the reader again maketh 3 prostrations. Upon the conclusion of the 3rd Antiphon the Priest saith nothing, but the cantor himself of his own accord singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord. Amen”; and the reader maketh 3 prostrations and departeth. And immediately the cantor beginneth to sing: “Lord, I have cried” with the stichera, and the Priest censeth the church. Stichera on 10: 3 penitential from the Oktoechos, 3 from the Triodion, and 4 unto the Saint (repeating the first sticheron);[^94] “Glory,” of the Saint, if there be one; if not: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion from the Menaion. On Fridays: “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” dogmaticon of the tone from the Oktoechos (for it is the apodosis of the tone). After the censing of the church, the Priest cometh unto the holy table, taketh the chalice out of the tabernacle, taketh the Holy Lamb, placeth It upon the diskos, covereth It with the star and the veil, and having censed It, carrieth It unto the prothesis with candle-bearers preceding. Here into the chalice he poureth wine and water, saying: “Let us pray unto the Lord.” And after this, having prepared everything, he returneth unto the Holy Table, and here at “Glory,” and “Now and” maketh the entrance with the censer (and at times also with the Gospel), and before the royal doors proclaimeth: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” After “Gladsome Light” and after “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” the 1st prokimenon is sung and there taketh place the reading from the Book of Genesis. Upon the conclusion of this reading the choir at once singeth the 2nd prokimenon, during which the Priest cometh unto the holy table, and when the cantor proclaimeth: “Command!” the Priest with the three-branched candle and with the censer, looking toward the East, saith: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” and having turned unto the people saith: “The light of Christ enlighteneth all.” Unto this the reader immediately beginneth the 2nd reading, saying: “The reading from the Proverbs,” whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend!” and the reader beginneth to read the 2nd paremia. After the paremias there is sung: “Let my prayer arise,” during which singing, when the verse “Let my prayer arise” is sung briskly, the Priest standeth; when, however, the verses “Let my prayer arise,” “Lord, I have cried,” “Set a watch, O Lord,” and “Incline not my heart” are sung slowly, the Priest falleth upon his knees. Upon the conclusion of this singing, 3 prostrations. (After this at times there followeth also a prokimenon, Epistle, and Gospel, that is to say, if a certain polyeleos Saint is celebrated.) After the Gospel, if there be one, but if not, then after the singing of “Let my prayer arise,” the litany “Let us all say” is sung, and thus the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated as the Liturgikon showeth, concerning which word will be made in Section II. (From this Vespers likewise only one half is taken, and that up to the paremias.)

§. 31.

Order of Great Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great

The Priest, having prepared himself as at other times for the celebration of the Liturgy, having come into the church, before the royal doors concludeth the prayers that are to be celebrated before the Divine Liturgy. Then, having entered into the sanctuary, he vesteth in all the priestly vestments, saying the prescribed prayers, washeth his hands, and immediately goeth unto the holy table. Here, having bowed, he beginneth secretly: “Blessed is our God,” then “Heavenly King,” “Glory to God in the highest,” etc. (twice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (once). After this, having kissed the holy table and the cross, he proclaimeth: “Blessed is the kingdom.” The choir answereth: “Amen,” and readeth: “Come, let us worship” (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” The Priest concludeth the evening prayers. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest loudly proclaimeth the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” After this litany the choir singeth: “Lord, I have cried,” during which singing the Priest departeth unto the table of preparation and concludeth the proskomedia. Stichera on 8 or on 10. After “Glory,” and “Now and,” entrance with the censer and the Gospel. “Gladsome Light.” Prokimenon of the day and 3 paremias, but at times even more.[^95] During the last reading the Priest ascendeth by the northern side unto the holy table and readeth the prayer of the Trisagion already from the Liturgy (“O Holy God, Who restest in the holy,” etc.). Upon the conclusion of the readings and the little litany, the Priest proclaimeth: “For holy art Thou,” etc., and thus followeth the Trisagion, or: “All of you that have been baptized into Christ.” And after this the Divine Liturgy of Basil is concluded according to the order that is prescribed in the Liturgikon, — concerning which word will be made in Section II.

Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great is celebrated on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ, and on the Paramony of Theophany, if it happen not on a Saturday or Sunday;[^96] likewise always on Holy and Great Thursday, and Great Saturday; furthermore, on the feast of the Annunciation with the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom, if this feast falleth in the Great Fast, Saturdays and Sundays excepted.

§. 32.

Order of Vespers of Holy and Great Friday with Procession

This Vespers is great with an entrance, with the Gospel, and with the great dismissal. It is renowned also for this reason, that it hath a procession around the church with the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*). The Priest beginneth it as usual in a black epitrachelion and concludeth it in due order. The usual beginning. No kathisma. Stichera on 6. Before the entrance the Priest vesteth not only in the epitrachelion and phelonion, but also in the sticharion, and thus maketh the entrance with the censer and with the Gospel. After “Gladsome Light” the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon is sung. After the prokimenon, 1 paremia, and again one prokimenon, and thus 2 paremias. After the paremias immediately: “Let us attend! Peace be to all!” and the prokimenon is sung, and the Epistle is read: to the Corinthians, pericope 125 [1 Cor. 1:18–2:2]. After the Epistle the Priest proclaimeth: “Peace be to thee,” whereunto the choir singeth: “Alleluia” (thrice), and the Priest readeth the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 110 [Matt. 27:1–38; Luke 23:39–43; Matt. 27:39–54; John 19:31–37; Matt. 27:55–61]. After this, the litany: “Let us all say.” “Vouchsafe, O Lord” is read, and the litany is sung: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Aposticha stichera. When the choir saith: “Now lettest Thou,” the Priest spreadeth out the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*) upon the holy table and censeth It. Thereafter, when the choir, upon the conclusion of “Now lettest Thou,” the Trisagion, and “Our Father,” beginneth to sing: “The noble Joseph,” the Priest placeth the Shroud upon his shoulders, and holding It with both hands upon his head, while other two men hold the other end of the Shroud, that is to say, from the feet, and thus the Priest, with the deacon censing and candle-bearers preceding, maketh the procession around the church, and that only once. At the procession Gospels also are read from all four Evangelists, namely: 1. Gospel from Matthew, pericope 113 (“Now when the even was come... over against the sepulchre” [Matt. 27:57–61]). 2. Gospel from Mark, pericope 66 (“Joseph came... where He was laid” [Mark 15:42–47]). 3. Gospel from Luke, pericope 111 (“And a man... rested according to the commandment” [Luke 23:50–56]). 4. Gospel from John, pericope 62 (“At that time... laid they Jesus” [John 19:38–42]). Having returned into the church, the Priest layeth the Shroud into the tomb, or upon another prepared place, and censeth It. And the cantor singeth at “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Unto the Myrrh-bearing Women.” After this, having put off the phelonion, he goeth unto the pulpit and delivereth the holy sermon. And after the sermon with genuflection he saith the prayer prescribed in the Trebnik. After the prayer, the great dismissal, namely: “Wisdom”; the choir: “Bless.” The Priest: “Blessed and glorified is...” After the blessing there taketh place the kissing of the Shroud with prostrations.

§. 33.

Order of Great Vespers with the Liturgy of St. Basil and with the Blessing of Loaves on Holy and Great Saturday

It beginneth at the 9th hour. The Priest, having come into the church, as usual as before the Divine Liturgy concludeth the prayers before the icons of the Savior and the Mother of God. After this, having entered into the holy sanctuary, he vesteth in all the priestly vestments, and those black, saying the prescribed prayers, and having washed his hands goeth unto the holy table and beginneth quietly: “Blessed is our God. Heavenly King. Glory to God in the highest” (twice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (once). After this he proclaimeth: “Blessed is the kingdom,” the choir: “Amen.” And immediately the cantor: “Come, let us worship” (thrice), Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,” “Alleluia” (thrice); during the singing the Priest readeth the evening prayers, and after “Alleluia (thrice), Glory to Thee, O God,” the litany of peace. After the Exclamation the choir singeth: “Lord, I have cried” with the stichera on 8. And the Priest departeth to celebrate the proskomedia. When “Glory,” and “Now and” approacheth, the proskomedia must be concluded; during “Glory,” and “Now and” the entrance taketh place with the censer and the Gospel. The Priest: “Wisdom! Stand upright!” the choir: “Gladsome Light,” etc.; the prokimenon is not said, but 6 readings follow. And immediately the Priest at each one proclaimeth: “Wisdom!” the choir: “The reading from Genesis”; thereafter “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah”; further “The reading from Exodus,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Jonah,” “The reading from Joshua,” “The reading from Exodus.” This 6th reading the reader concludeth with the words: “Then sang Moses and the children of Israel this song unto the Lord, and spoke, saying,” whereunto the reader in Tone 5 beginneth to lead the chanting of the following verse: “Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.” The reader: “Glory,” and “Now and.” The choir: “Let us sing unto the Lord.” And again the reader concludeth: “Let us sing unto the Lord,” etc. After this follow 9 readings. And that: the Priest: “Wisdom!” the reader: “The reading from the Prophecy of Zephaniah,” “The reading from the Third Book of Kings,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from Genesis,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from the Sixth Book of Kings,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah,” “The reading from the Prophecy of Daniel.” And immediately the reader subjoineth the prayer or Song of the Three Holy Children: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised...” etc. At the very end of this the reader singeth the verse: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all forever,” and the choir singeth it twice. And immediately the reader chanteth the verses: “Bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord, we praise,” and the choir after each verse answereth: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him above all forever.” At the end the reader: “Glory... and now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen”; the choir: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him,” etc. The reader: “Praise the Lord and exalt Him,” etc. After this followeth the little litany with the Exclamation. During this singing the Priest from the high place entereth unto the Holy Table and here quietly saith the prayer prescribed in the Liturgikon before the Trisagion, and proclaimeth: “For holy art Thou,” etc. Instead of the Trisagion: “All of you that have been baptized into Christ.” Afterwards the Priest: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir the prokimenon: “Let all the earth worship Thee,” etc. The Epistle (to the Romans, pericope 91 [Rom. 6:3–11]). After this the Priest: “Peace be to thee! Wisdom, let us attend!” whereunto the choir instead of the Alleluia[^97] saith: “Arise, O God, judge the earth, for Thou shalt inherit among all the nations” [Ps. 81:8] multiple times in Tone 7. During that time the Priest changeth the black vestments into bright ones, and there followeth the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 115 [Matt. 28:1–20]. After this everything followeth in the order of the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. Instead of the Cherubic Hymn there is sung: “Let all mortal flesh keep silence,” and at the “Especially”: “Weep not for Me, O Mother”; the Communion Hymn: “The Lord awoke as one out of sleep, and is risen, saving us. Alleluia.” After the Divine Liturgy there is performed the blessing of loaves, wheat, wine, and oil, as at a Litiya. That is to say, after the Ambo Prayer the Priest cometh into the midst of the church, and having taken his stand there before the tetrapod proclaimeth: “Let us pray unto the Lord,” and loudly reciteth the prayer: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves,” etc.; after the prayer he censeth, and upon the conclusion of the singing of “Blessed be the name of the Lord” maketh the dismissal.

§. 34.

Order of Vespers in Bright Week

(α) Order of Vespers on Easter Sunday with the Gospel. — For this Vespers the Priest vesteth in all the priestly vestments, and since throughout all Bright Week the royal doors are open, he censeth, and cometh unto the Holy Table, where with the censer he signeth cruciformly and proclaimeth, saying: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this the Priest loudly singeth: “Christ is risen,” etc., once, and the choir twice. And immediately the Priest singeth the following verses: “Let God arise,” etc., “As smoke vanisheth,” and the rest, and the choir after each verse singeth: “Christ is risen”; at the end the Priest singeth “Christ is risen” once, and the choir twice. Then the Priest saith the litany: “In peace let us pray unto the Lord.” And immediately the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” on 6. At “Glory,” and “Now and” the entrance taketh place with the Gospel. At “Wisdom! Stand upright!”: “Gladsome Light.” “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and immediately the Great Prokimenon. After the prokimenon: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Peace be to all,” etc., “Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel,” etc. (from John, pericope 65: “The same day at evening,” etc. [John 20:19–25]). The Gospel is read from the royal doors, and after it followeth the holy sermon. After this the litany: “Let us all say,” etc., “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” etc., the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. After this litany the choir singeth this aposticha sticheron: “Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior,” etc.; and the Priest immediately, having taken the Gospel and the Cross, cometh forth onto the ambo and singeth: “Let God arise” and the other verses. Unto this the choir after each verse singeth the Paschal stichera: “A sacred Pascha,” etc., and concludeth with “Christ is risen” thrice. During the singing the people kiss the Cross, the Gospel, and the icon of the risen Christ. Likewise at that time the myrovania taketh place and the blessed bread, or so-called antidoron, is distributed. After that the great dismissal taketh place, that is to say: “Wisdom,” “Bless,” etc., “Christ is risen from the dead, trampling down death by death, and unto those in the tombs bestowing life, our true God,” etc., as in the Molitvoslov, p. 478. After the dismissal the Priest, having turned unto the people with the Cross, saith thrice: “Christ is risen!” and the people answer: “Truly He is risen!” Blessing. And thus he celebrateth Vespers throughout the entire Bright Week. In Bright Week every blessing taketh place with the Cross.

(β) Order of Vespers in Bright Week, if on a certain day a polyeleos Saint should occur. — The Priest, having put on all the vestments and censed the church, before the Holy Table beginneth from: “Blessed is our God.” After this: “Christ is risen” thrice with the verses. At the end the Priest singeth: “Christ is risen” once, and the choir twice. And immediately the litany of peace. (There is no kathisma in Bright Week.) At “Lord, I have cried,” 6 stichera, namely 3 of the Resurrection, and 3 unto the Saint. “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion-dogmaticon, that is, the first of the tone. Entrance with the censer. Prokimenon of the day. Readings of the Saint. At the aposticha, one sticheron of the Resurrection, and the rest the stichera of Pascha. (Veneration of the Cross.) “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” “The Day of Resurrection,” etc., “Christ is risen” thrice. And immediately “Glory,” Troparion unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. Paschal dismissal with the Cross. Blessing with the Cross throughout the entire week.

Note. Concerning Vespers from Thomas Sunday until Ascension, it is necessary to note the following regarding these: (α) Sunday Vespers, sung on Saturday evening, is celebrated thus: After “Blessed is our God” the Priest singeth “Christ is risen” once, and the cantor twice. (The verses are not sung.) And immediately the Trisagion.[^98] “Our Father” with the Exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (thrice). The Introductory Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” Litany of peace. Kathisma. At “Lord, I have cried,” 10 stichera.[^99] Great entrance.

2 litanies. At the aposticha, one sticheron of the Resurrection in order from the Flowery Triodion, and immediately the stichera of Pascha are sung (except on Thomas Sunday): “Let God arise,” etc., “A sacred Pascha,” etc. “Glory,” of the Saint, if there be one; “Now and,” “The Day of Resurrection,” and once: “Christ is risen”; and thus “Now Thou lettest depart.” (When the stichera of Pascha are sung, the Priest openeth the royal doors, cometh forth onto the ambo, and giveth the Cross unto the people to kiss.) After “Now Thou lettest depart” the troparia are sung and immediately the dismissal of Pascha with the Cross. — (β) Concerning daily Vespers, it is necessary to note that the beginning of this Vespers is the same as that of Sunday Vespers. It is celebrated in order. After the litany, the kathisma of the day; the stichera at “Lord, I have cried” on 6, and that also unto the Saint. There is no entrance. The stichera at the aposticha are of the Resurrection from the Flowery Triodion (and not of Pascha). “Glory,” unto the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion. After “Now Thou lettest depart,” all in order. (The stichera of Pascha at daily Vespers are taken only on Tuesday evening at the Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha.) The dismissal is without the Cross, that is to say: “Wisdom”; “He Who rose from the dead,” etc.

§. 35.

Order of Vespers on the Descent of the Holy Spirit

This Vespers on Sunday of the Descent of the Holy Spirit is celebrated immediately after the Divine Liturgy, and that together with the kneeling prayers for the invocation of the Holy Spirit. The order of this Vespers is as follows: After “Blessed be the name of the Lord” the Priest, having entered into the holy altar, beginneth: “Blessed is our God,” etc., and saith “Heavenly King,” the Trisagion, and the rest of the ordinary beginning, and the Psalm: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,” etc. After “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest chanteth the litany of peace, unto which after the petition: “For the people here present” he appendeth also the special litany “For the invocation of the help of the Holy Spirit” found in the *Sluzhebnik*, which he reciteth with kneeling. After the exclamation: “For unto Thee is due,” the choir singeth: “Lord, I have cried” and the stichera. (There is no kathisma.) “Glory,” and “Now and.” Entrance with the censer. Great Prokimenon: “Who is a God as great as our God?” etc. After the prokimenon the Priest ascendeth onto the ambo and proclaimeth: “Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray unto the Lord,” and having bowed his knees, reciteth the 1st prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc. After this, having arisen, he saith the small litany: “Help us, save us,” etc., and then the litany: “Let us all say.” — After the litany he proclaimeth: “Again and again, having bowed our knees,” etc., and reciteth the 2nd prayer:

“O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc. After this prayer he chanteth again the small litany: “Help us, save us,” etc., and immediately the choir readeth: “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” After this the Priest again saith: “Again and again, having bowed our knees,” etc., and reciteth the 3rd prayer: “O ever-flowing, life-creating,” etc. After this: “Help us, save us,” etc., and the litany: “Let us complete our evening prayer,” etc. Stichera at the aposticha. “Now Thou lettest depart.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Troparion of the Holy Trinity, and “Glory,” and “Now and,” of the Feast. Proper festal dismissal.

§. 36.

Order of Vespers on the Paramony of the Nativity of Jesus Christ, if it should occur on a Saturday or on a Sunday

On the Paramony of the Nativity of Jesus Christ, the Royal Hours conclude in order in the morning together with Vespers and the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. But if the Paramony should fall on a Saturday or on a Sunday, the celebration is divided, and that thus: the Royal Hours are celebrated beforehand on Friday; Vespers is celebrated on the Paramony itself (that is, on Saturday or on Sunday) in the evening at the 3rd hour with the evening paremias, with the troparia, and with the Epistle and Gospel of the Paramony; while the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil is celebrated on the very day of the Nativity of Christ. — Concerning this Great Vespers we note that it hath the ordinary beginning. Stichera at “Lord, I have cried” of the Nativity on 8. Entrance with the censer and with the Gospel. After “Gladsome Light” the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon of the day is sung, after which there are 8 readings with troparia. These troparia are inserted at the 3rd and after the 6th paremia. At the conclusion of the 8th reading, the small litany, and immediately: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the prokimenon of the Epistle is taken: “The Lord said unto Me” [Ps. 2:7]. — The Epistle is read, pericope 303 [Gal. 3:15–22]. Alleluia and the Gospel from Matthew, pericope 53. Then “Let us all say,” “Vouchsafe, O Lord,” and “Let us complete our evening prayer,” and immediately the great dismissal. — (There are no stichera at the aposticha.)
Note. In like manner the celebration of the Paramony of Theophany is divided, if it should occur on a Saturday or Sunday. There, in addition, it is also necessary to note that this Vespers in this case is not only similar to this one, but in such a case it is also combined with the blessing of water. — That is to say, after the readings, after the Epistle and Gospel and after both litanies, the Priest cometh forth into the midst of the church, where water is prepared, and the choir beginneth to sing: “The voice of the Lord is upon the waters,” and the blessing of water is celebrated, concerning which mention will be made in Article III.

ARTICLE II.

§. 37.

On Complines

Ordinary Complines are divided: (α) into small, and (β) into great. And of the extraordinary complines the most notable are: (α) Small Compline without a canon; (β) Complines in the Great Fast; (γ) Compline with Litiya.
Small Compline among us is celebrated publicly in church only on Holy Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday, and on the Descent of the Holy Spirit; but privately at home throughout the entire year, even in the Great Fast on Saturday and on Sunday (that is, for Sunday and for Monday). Great Compline, however, is celebrated publicly: (α) on Tuesday and on Thursday (that is, for Wednesday and for Friday) of Cheesefare Week; (β) on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday (with the exception of Wednesday of the 5th week), Thursday, and Friday (with the exception of Friday of the 5th week) of all weeks of the Great Fast; (γ) on Monday and Tuesday of Passion Week; (δ) on the Paramony of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany; (ε) on the Paramony of the Annunciation, if that feast should occur on Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Saturday of the first 6 weeks of the Great Fast, or if it should occur on Tuesday, Wednesday, or Thursday of Passion Week.

1) Ordinary Complines.

§. 38.

Order of Small Compline

If this Divine Service is sung in church, then the Priest in epitrachelion before the royal doors maketh the ordinary beginning, that is to say, proclaimeth: “Blessed is our God,” etc., unto which the choir answereth: “Amen.”

And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Priest entereth into the holy altar, and the choir readeth the Psalms, namely the 50th (“Have mercy on me, O God” [Ps. 50]), the 69th [Ps. 69], and the 142nd (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]); and after the Psalms it readeth: “Glory to God in the highest.” The Creed. After this there is sung the daily canon in honor of the Most Holy Theotokos from the Octoechos.[^100] (Katavasia is not sung after each ode.) After the 3rd ode no litany is sung, but there is said: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” and the Sessional Hymn. After the 6th ode likewise: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” Kontakion and Ikos. At the conclusion of the 9th ode both choirs together sing: “It is truly right.”[^101] After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. On Saturday, the Hypakoe of the tone; if it be a feast or a commemorated Saint, his Kontakion; but if it be a simple day, the troparion of the temple, of the day, and the present one: “O God of our Fathers,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 271). “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). During this the Priest cometh forth and before the royal doors reciteth the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. The choir: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. And after “Amen” he proclaimeth the prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc., and subjoineth the prayer: “And grant unto us,” etc., with “O most glorious,” and “My hope,” etc. After this the troparia are sung: “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc. “Glory,” “O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc. “Now and,” “Open unto us the doors of compassion,” etc. After these, the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., “Again we pray that this city may be preserved...” and after the exclamation: “Hear us, O God our Savior...” The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God.” After this, the commemorations.[^102] If the morrow be a Sunday, a feast, or a commemorated Saint, then the Priest after “For the abundance of the fruits of the earth” doth not commemorate the departed, but only the living, and therefore immediately followeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and thus the blessing taketh place.

§. 39.

Order of Great Daily Compline

Great Compline is divided into 3 parts: (α) thanksgiving, (β) penitential, and (γ) doxological.

Part I. The Priest in epitrachelion maketh the ordinary beginning, saying: “Blessed is our God,” etc. The choir: “Amen.” And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Come, let us worship” (thrice). During this the Priest entereth into the holy altar, and the cantor beginneth to read the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” Again the Psalms: 24, 30, 90. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). After the Psalms the Priest cometh forth and loudly chanteth the hymn: “God is with us, understand, all ye nations, and submit yourselves, for God is with us”; the choir chanteth the same twice. After this the Priest chanteth the verses, and the choir repeateth after each verse: “God is with us.” At the conclusion of this singing the Priest entereth, and the cantor readeth: “The day being past,” etc. “The bodiless.” The Creed. “Most Holy.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation, and immediately the troparia are sung. After the troparia the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times) (and the Priest cometh forth). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” etc., concludeth the 1st part, having entered into the altar.
Part II of Great Compline beginneth with the invitation: “Come, let us worship” (thrice); the Psalms: 50, 101. After the Psalms the Priest cometh forth and reciteth the prayer of King Manasses: “O Lord Almighty.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Kontakion of the feast or of the day. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, Lord Almighty,” etc., concludeth the 2nd part, and goeth into the holy altar.
Part III. This part beginneth with the invitation: “Come, let us worship” (thrice); the Psalms: the 69th (“O God, come to my assistance” [Ps. 69]) and the 142nd (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]). After this: “Glory to God in the highest” is only read. A canon, sometimes even two.[^103] After the canon there is sung the hymn: “It is truly right.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this followeth the troparion: “O Lord of hosts,” etc., with Psalm 150: “Praise God in His saints” [Ps. 150:1], which is sung in such a manner that unto each verse of that Psalm there is appended as a refrain: “O Lord of hosts”; and at “Glory” there is sung: “O Lord, if we had not Thy saints,” etc., and at “Now and”: “A multitude of transgressions,” etc. After this: “O Most Holy Theotokos.” “All my hope.” After this followeth “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). And the Priest cometh forth and reciteth the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc., and the choir:

“Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And immediately the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. After “God be merciful unto us” immediately the prayer: “O undefiled, unblemished,” etc., and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” etc., “My hope,” and the rest, likewise as at Small Compline.

2) Extraordinary Complines.

§. 40.

Order of Small Compline without a Canon

The beginning of this Compline is ordinary, with the Psalms and with “I believe in one God,” etc. — After the Creed there is no canon, but immediately “It is truly right,” and immediately the Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this the troparion of the temple, of the day, and “O God of our Fathers.” After the troparia: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc. After this: “O undefiled,” and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope.” Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. Dismissal. Commemorations.
There is no canon at Compline: (α) on the days of Meatfare and Cheesefare Weeks, except Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week; (β) on the day of the Annunciation, if it should occur in the Great Fast. Then in the morning Great Compline with Litiya is celebrated, and in the evening Small Compline without a canon;[^104] (γ) on the days of Bright Week; (δ) on the days from Monday of the Holy Spirit even unto the Sunday of All Saints.
Note. The Compline of Bright Week is given in the *Flowery Triodion*.

§. 41.

Order of Complines in the Holy Great Fast

(α) Order of Small Compline with Prostrations. — This Compline the Priest must read in his own house on Sundays of the Great Fast. It is the Compline of Monday.[^105] It is celebrated thus: Ordinary beginning: “Blessed is our God,” etc. Come, let us worship (thrice). The Psalms: 50, 69, and 142. After this: “Glory to God in the highest,” the Creed. The Theotokion canon. After the canon: “It is truly right.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. And immediately: the troparia of the temple, of the day, and the present one: “O God of our Fathers.”[^106] After the troparia the choir saith: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times); the Priest, having come forth, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” The choir: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” The Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and maketh 3 great prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem (“O Lord and Master of my life”). After this, the Trisagion (thrice), “O Most Holy Trinity,” “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). The prayer: “O undefiled,” etc., “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope,” etc. The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.” The commemorations, and thus the blessing. (The troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” are omitted.)
(β) Order of Great Compline in the First Week of the Holy Great Fast. — Part I. The Priest in epitrachelion beginneth, saying: “Blessed is our God,” unto which the choir: “Amen.” And immediately: “Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Come, let us worship” (thrice). Then the Priest entereth into the holy altar, and the cantor readeth the 69th Psalm (“O God, come to my assistance” [Ps. 69]). After the Psalm, the Great Canon of St. Andrew of Crete.[^107] After the canon “It is truly right” is not sung, but instead of it the 9th ode is repeated, and one prostration even unto the ground is made. After this the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and,” and immediately the Psalms: 24, 30, 90. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). And the Priest, having come forth, chanteth the hymn: “God is with us,” the choir twice. The Priest chanteth the verses, the cantor appendeth after each verse: “God is with us.” After this hymn the Priest entereth into the holy altar, and the cantor readeth: “The day being past,” etc., “The bodiless nature,” etc., “Most Holy Virgin.” The Creed. “O Most Holy Lady, pray to God for us sinners” — twice; while the 1st choir reciteth, the other maketh a prostration, — and while the other choir ariseth and answereth, the 1st choir maketh a prostration. Likewise also at the other verses.[^108] After these petitions, the Trisagion, “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this on Monday and Wednesday the troparia: “Enlighten mine eyes,” and on Tuesday and Thursday: “Mine unseen enemies.”[^109] After these troparia: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest before the royal doors proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and reciteth the prayer: “O Lord, Lord, Who deliverest us,” etc., and herewith the 1st part of this Compline is concluded. — Part II beginneth with “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Psalms: 50, 101 (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 101]). After these Psalms the Priest reciteth the prayer: “O Lord Almighty,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. And immediately we sing the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” and reciteth the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., with which at once he also concludeth the 2nd part. Part III beginneth with “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this, since Psalm 69, “O God, come to my assistance,” is omitted, immediately the 142nd Psalm is taken (“O Lord, hear my prayer” [Ps. 142]). After the Psalm: “Glory to God in the highest.” Then: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this is sung loudly and slowly: “O Lord of hosts, be with us,” with the verses at the Praises. “Glory,” “O Lord, if we had not,” “Now and,” “A multitude of transgressions.” Then: “O Most Holy Theotokos.” “All my hope.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. After the prostrations, the Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and the prayers (kneeling): “O undefiled,” and “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope.” After this the smallest dismissal from: “Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.” The choir: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Bless.” The Priest, however, instead of the dismissal saith the Litiya prayer: “O most merciful Master.” At the conclusion of this prayer immediately follow the commemorations and the blessing.
(γ) Order of Great Compline in the Remaining Weeks of the Great Fast. — This Compline is similar to that previously described, differing from it only in that the canon is recited at the end. It is celebrated thus: Part I. “Blessed is our God,” and the rest of the ordinary beginning. After “Come, let us worship” (thrice), the Psalms: 4, 6, 12. “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Psalms: 24, 30, and the 90th. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” The singing: “God is with us.” The petitions: “The day being past, I give thanks,” etc. “Glory,” “The day being past, I glorify,” etc. “Now and,” “The day being past, I hymn,” etc. “The bodiless.” “Most Holy Virgin.” The Creed. “O Most Holy Lady.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation, and immediately the troparia, on Monday and Wednesday: “Enlighten mine eyes,” on Tuesday and Thursday: “Mine unseen enemies.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and reciteth the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” with which he concludeth the 1st part. Part II beginneth with “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Psalms: 50 and the 101st. The prayer of King Manasses: “O Lord Almighty,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludeth the 2nd part.
Part III beginneth from “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Psalms: 69 and the 142nd. “Glory to God in the highest.” The canon in honor of the Most Holy Theotokos. “It is truly right.” When the choir concludeth: “It is truly right,” the Priest, having come forth before the royal doors, maketh one prostration (except on Friday). After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “O Lord of hosts,”[^110] with the verses at the Praises. “Glory,” “O Lord, if we had not.” “Now and,” “A multitude of transgressions,” and the rest. “Lord, have mercy” (50 times). “Thou Who at all times.” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” and the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and maketh the prostrations. After the prostrations, the Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and the Priest, having bowed his knees, saith the prayers: “O undefiled,” “And grant unto us,” “O most glorious,” “Our hope,” and after this immediately saith the smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc. The choir: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Bless,” unto which the Priest saith instead of the dismissal the Litiya prayer: “O most merciful Master,” etc. After the prayer, the commemorations and the blessing.
Note. It must be noted that at the end of Compline the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Again we pray that this city may be preserved,” etc., in the Great Fast are taken neither at Small nor at Great Compline, except in Passion Week on Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday at Small Compline, as is our custom.
(δ) Order of Great Compline on the Fridays of the Great Fast.[^111] This Compline is without prostrations, for in the Greek Church there are no prostrations on Saturday. It is celebrated thus: Part I. Ordinary beginning with the Psalms, with “God is with us,” and with the other petitions even unto “Our Father.” After this the troparion is taken, namely: on Friday of the 1st week, the troparion of the Holy Martyr Theodore; on Fridays of the 2nd, 3rd, and 4th weeks of the Great Fast, the troparion to the Martyrs and for the Departed; on Friday of the 5th week there is Small Compline with the troparia of the Akathist and of the Great Canon; on Friday of the 6th week there is the troparion of St. Lazarus. After the given troparion followeth: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord,” concludeth the 1st part. Part II is celebrated in the ordinary manner even unto “Our Father.” After this the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Then: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable.” The Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God,” etc., concludeth the 2nd part.
Part III beginneth from “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this the Psalms: 67 and the 142nd. “Glory to God in the highest.” The canon for the departed of the current tone, sometimes even two canons. “It is truly right,” but without prostrations. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “O Lord of hosts”[^112] we do not sing, but instead of “O Lord of hosts” the kontakia are taken, namely: on Friday of the 1st week, the kontakion of the Holy Martyr Theodore; on Fridays of the 2nd, 3rd, and 4th weeks, the kontakion for the departed: “With the Saints give rest”; on Friday of the 5th week and on Wednesday of the 5th week, since then there is Small Compline, the kontakia of the Saturday of the Akathist and of the Great Canon; on Friday of the 6th week, the kontakion of St. Lazarus. After the kontakion: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Thou Who at all times,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., but maketh no prostrations. After the prostrations there follow (the Trisagion, “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” [12 times] are omitted) the prayers: “O undefiled, unblemished,” and “And grant unto us,” “Our hope,” but without kneeling. (The prayer “O most merciful Master” is omitted.) The smallest dismissal, commemorations, and the blessing.

§. 42.

Order of Great Compline with Litiya.

The Priest in phelonion and epitrachelion censeth the holy sanctuary, openeth the royal doors, censeth the whole church, standeth before the royal doors, and when the chanter saith: “Command!” he beginneth: “O Lord, bless!” And he proclaimeth: “Glory to the holy...” — and thus followeth: “Come, let us worship.” The Priest, having entered after this into the holy sanctuary, shutteth the royal doors, and having divested of the phelonion, in the epitrachelion cometh forth before the royal doors. The chanter meanwhile beginneth Part I of Great Compline, saying after “Come, let us worship” 3 psalms. The Priest goeth into the holy sanctuary, and the chanter readeth Psalms: 4, 6, 12 [Pss. 4, 6, 12]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And immediately Psalms: 24, 30, and the 90th [Pss. 24, 30, 90]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And immediately the Priest cometh forth and singeth once: “God is with us.” The choir repeateth this 1st verse twice. After this the Priest, standing before the royal doors, readeth the verses: “Unto the ends of the earth,” etc., and the choir after each verse singeth: “God is with us,” etc. At the end the Priest again singeth this first verse once, and the choir repeateth it twice. After this canticle of the Prophet Isaiah there followeth: “The day being past,” etc., “The bodiless nature,” “O Most Holy Virgin,” “I believe,” “O Most Holy Trinity,” each verse twice. After these: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast (for instance, of the Nativity of Christ, the Theophany, or the Annunciation), and that once. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc. And the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Lord, Lord of hosts,” concludeth Part I, having entered into the sanctuary. Part II beginneth from “Come, let us worship” (thrice), after which follow Psalms: 50 and the 101st [Pss. 50, 101]. The prayer of King Manasses, which the Priest readeth before the royal doors. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaimeth: “O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., and with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludeth Part II, having entered into the holy sanctuary. Part III beginneth from “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this Psalms: 69 and the 142nd [Pss. 69, 142]. “Glory to God in the highest” is read. — Meanwhile the Priest vesteth in the phelonion, openeth the royal doors, blesseth the censer, and in the usual manner, as at the entrance, goeth forth by the northern door, censeth the entire church, and after the censing goeth unto the table or *Tetrapod* in the midst of the church, where there are already prepared 5 loaves or prosphora, wheat, wine, and oil. The arrangement of the *Tetrapod* is thus: on the right side is placed the wine, on the left the oil, in the middle the wheat, and upon it 5 loaves (prosphora), and that four prosphora are placed cruciformly, and the fifth lieth upon them touching all four. After this the choir after “Glory to God in the highest,” etc., singeth the stichera at the Litiya (and that 5 with “Glory,” and “Now and”), and upon the conclusion of these the Priest chanteth the litany at the Litiya: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this, the stichera at the aposticha. Next: “Now Thou lettest depart.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast (thrice). (When the Priest singeth the troparion, he at the same time also censeth.)
