After the troparion the Priest proclaimeth: “Let us pray to the Lord,” and readeth the prayer over the blessing of the loaves: “O Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., taking first the prosphora lying on top, and having then set it back in its place, with outstretched fingers he signeth the entire oblation at the words: “Bless these loaves, the wheat, the wine, and the oil” with a single sign of the cross. After this prayer followeth: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (thrice), while the Priest censeth the oblation, — and after this Psalm 33 [Ps. 33] is read up to “Shall not want any good thing.” The Priest goeth unto the royal doors, and the chanter upon the conclusion of the psalm saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; and the Priest standing before the royal doors, at the exclamation of the choir: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” beginneth Matins, saying: “Glory to the holy...” etc.[^113]

It is necessary to note that if Compline is combined with Litiya, then from Compline everything beginning from the canon is omitted.

### ARTICLE III.

§. 43.

On the Midnight Offices.

The ordinary Midnight Office is threefold: (α) daily, (β) Saturday, and (γ) Sunday.

The daily Midnight Office is read on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday. — The Saturday one, however, only on Saturday; and the Sunday one on Sundays of the whole year whereon there is no vigil.

Besides the ordinary Midnight Offices there are also extraordinary ones. Such are: (α) in the Great Fast; (β) on feasts; (γ) on Holy and Great Saturday.

### 1) Ordinary Midnight Offices.

§. 44.

Order of the Daily Midnight Office.

On weekdays it is usually read in private, but sometimes it is also sung in church before Matins. — If we sing this Midnight Office in church, the Priest in the epitrachelion before the royal doors maketh the usual beginning, proclaiming: “Blessed is our God,” etc. After this: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice). And the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The 17th Kathisma, which hath 3 antiphons. Before the 2nd and 3rd antiphons: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” After this: “I believe.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Troparia: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” “Glory,” “That day,” and “Now and,” “Thee, the invincible rampart”; and immediately “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). After this the Priest cometh forth and readeth the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest immediately proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., whereunto the choir answereth: “Amen.” After this the Priest with the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” etc., concludeth Part I.

Part II beginneth from: “Come, let us worship” (thrice), and immediately Psalms: 120 and the 133rd [Pss. 120, 133]. “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After the exclamation follow the troparia for the departed: “Remember, O Lord,” “With profound wisdom,” “Glory,” “With the Saints give rest,” “Now and,” “All generations bless thee,” etc. After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), whereunto the Priest readeth the prayer: “Remember, O Lord.” There are added also the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 47). After these, the smallest dismissal. Commemorations: “Let us pray for our most holy Hierarch,” etc. Furthermore after the commemorations the Priest proclaimeth aloud the prayers: “Remit, forgive,” “Those who hate,” etc., and giveth the blessing.

Note: In our *Molitvoslov* there are after the prayer “For the departed” also the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” further “Remit, forgive,” etc., and “Those who hate and wrong us,” etc., but all this is not contained in the Horologia.[^114]

§. 45.

Order of the Saturday Midnight Office.

The Saturday Midnight Office is celebrated in the same manner as the daily one, except that: (α) instead of the 17th Kathisma the 9th Kathisma is taken, that is to say, Psalms: 64, 65, 66, 67, 68, and the 69th [Pss. 64, 65, 66, 67, 68, 69]; (β) instead of the troparia: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” etc., there are read the troparia in honor of the Most Holy Trinity: “Uncreated nature”; finally (γ) instead of the prayer: “Those who hate,” etc., the prayer of St. Eustratius is taken: “Magnifying, I magnify Thee,” etc.

§. 46.

Order of the Sunday Midnight Office.

If it be celebrated in church, then: The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, maketh before the royal doors the usual beginning: “Blessed is our God,” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice). During this the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary, and the chanter beginneth to read Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this (instead of the kathisma), the canon to the Most Holy Trinity, which is found in the Octoechos under each tone. After the canon, first the Triadicon Megalynarion, consisting of several troparia (“It is truly meet to praise Thee, Who art God,” “The Chief of the Cherubim,” “Unto the unoriginate Father and God,” “Let us all sing,” “With radiant,” “Before Whom tremble,” “It is truly meet reverently,” “Receive tears,” “Glory,” “O Father Almighty,” and at the end “Now and,” “From the dead”). After this is sung the Megalynarion of the Theotokos: “It is truly right,” and thus: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation and (among us) the Troparia:[^115] “Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us,” and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., with the exclamation: “Hear us, O God.” After the litany the smallest dismissal, and that the resurrectional one (“Christ, our true God, Who rose from the dead,” etc.). Commemorations. (The commemoration for the departed is omitted.) The prayer: “Remit, forgive,” etc., and the prayer to the Most Holy Trinity: “All-powerful and life-creating,” and thus the blessing.

If, however, the Sunday Midnight Office is read in private, then there is read the general Triadicon canon, recorded in the *Molitvoslov*, and at the end after the troparia “Have mercy on us, O Lord” the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., is not said, but instead of it: “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Lord, bless,” and the Dismissal. And thus the prayer: “All-powerful and life-creating,” etc.

### 2) Extraordinary Midnight Offices.

§. 47.

Order of the Daily Midnight Office in the Holy Great Fast.

It is celebrated in the same manner as the daily Midnight Office at other times, except that it is necessary to note that at the end of Part I, after “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and before the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” there are prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem: “O Lord and Master,” etc. And that is: (α) three great prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. (A great prostration is when a man falleth upon his knees and boweth his head unto the earth as far as he can.) (β) 12 lesser prostrations (with the verses: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner”; “O God, cleanse my sins and have mercy on me”; and “Without number have I sinned, O Lord, forgive me”). After the prayer: “O Master, God the Father,” again one great prostration. The rest in the usual manner.

§. 48.

Order of the Festal Midnight Office.

If a certain feast of the Lord, or a feast of the Mother of God, or a feast of a great Saint, or the Afterfeast of the Nativity of Christ and the Theophany fall upon weekdays whereon Litiya is not celebrated: then the Midnight Office is celebrated in the same manner as the daily Midnight Office, except that: (α) the troparion: “Behold, the Bridegroom,” etc., is omitted, and instead of these the troparion of the feast is sung; (β) the troparion and prayer for the departed are omitted, and instead of them the kontakion of the feast is sung. Upon the conclusion of the kontakion there are sung among us the Troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc., and the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., with the exclamation: “Hear us, O God.”[^116] Dismissal. Commemorations. (The commemoration for the departed is omitted.) Blessing. (The last two prayers are omitted.)

§. 49.

Order of the Midnight Office on Holy and Great Saturday.

Before the Resurrection of Christ, likewise before midnight, there is to be celebrated the Midnight Office, or the so-called “Burial Service” (*Nadhrobne*). And that in the following order: The Priest, vested in a black epitrachelion, before the royal doors beginneth the Midnight Office with: “Blessed is our God.” After this the choir: “Amen,” “O Heavenly King,” “Our Father,” “Come, let us worship” (thrice). Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. And there followeth the Matins canon of Great Saturday: “By a wave of the sea.” After the canon, instead of “It is truly right,” the Heirmos: “Weep not for Me, O Mother.” And immediately: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Troparia: “When Thou didst descend,” “The noble Joseph,” and “Unto the Myrrhbearers.” After these the Priest goeth from the royal doors unto the Tomb, and having bowed, censeth the Shroud (*Plashchanytsia*), and after this transferreth It unto the holy table, where he spreadeth It out and again censeth It. After this he saith the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., as at the Sunday Midnight Office. Dismissal.

### ARTICLE IV.

§. 50.

On Matins.

Ordinary Matins is divided into: (α) Small, that is to say, daily without the Great Doxology; and (β) Great, with the Great Doxology.

Daily Matins is celebrated on weekdays if there occur on them one or more minor Saints.

Great Matins, however, is celebrated on feasts and on Sundays. And in accordance with this it is: Festal and Sunday. But since feasts are of two kinds: those with the Doxology and those with the Polyeleos, so also festal Matins services are of two kinds, that is to say: (α) with the Doxology, but without the Polyeleos; (β) with both the Doxology and the Polyeleos.

Besides these ordinary Matins services there are also extraordinary ones, whereof the most notable are the following: (α) Matins for the departed; (β) Matins in the Great Fast; (γ) Matins with the Akathist; (δ) Matins in Passion Week; (ε) Matins with the Stases on Holy and Great Saturday; (ζ) Matins in Bright Week; (η) Matins on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.

### 1) Ordinary Matins Services.

§. 51.

Order of Small Daily Matins.

If there be no Midnight Office in church,[^117] then the Priest, having entered into the church, maketh three bows before the royal doors, and having kissed and put on the epitrachelion, beginneth in the usual manner: “Blessed is our God.” The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “O Heavenly King,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), “Come, let us worship” (thrice). Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20], after which: “Glory,” and “Now and.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. And after this the reader saith the troparion: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc., “Glory,” “Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross,” etc., “Now and,” Theotokion: “O fearsome defense,” etc. Upon the conclusion of these troparia the Priest proclaimeth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., “Furthermore let us pray for our most devout Emperor,” etc., unto which petitions the choir answereth: “Lord, have mercy” (6 times). Then the exclamation: “For a merciful and man-befriending God art Thou,” etc. Whereunto the choir answereth: “Amen,” and immediately proclaimeth aloud: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “Glory to the holy, consubstantial, and life-creating...” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and beginneth first: “Glory to God in the highest” (thrice), after this: “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). And in order it saith the Six Psalms (that is to say, Psalms: 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, and the 142nd [Pss. 3, 37, 62, 87, 102, 142]) in a quiet voice. After the recitation of three Psalms: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” Upon the completion of the Six Psalms: “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God” (the third time with chanting). After this the Priest, standing before the royal doors, saith the litany of peace: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” After its exclamation the Priest singeth: “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc., and the chanter repeateth it 4 times (according to the verses). The Priest, having bowed, withdraweth unto his place, and there secretly reciteth the morning prayers; and the chanter beginneth to sing the troparia, and that: the troparion of the Saint (twice); “Glory,” and “Now and,” the dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint (or as the typikon prescribeth). After the troparia followeth: Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn with the Theotokion. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. (Before the kathisma, both the 1st and the 2nd, the choir saith: “Lord, have mercy” [thrice] and “Glory,” and the reader continueth from “Now and.” After the kathisma: “Lord, have mercy” [twice], “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen.”) After the 2nd Sessional Hymn and after “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” the chanter readeth Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canons, usually 3 (two from the Octoechos, and one of the Saint). The troparia in the canons are read with the appropriate refrains, and that: let there be 14 troparia in all with “Glory,” and with “Now and.” The heirmoi are taken from the first canon. The heirmoi from the canon of the Saint are taken as the Katavasia, but only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th odes. After the 3rd ode there is the small litany and the Sessional Hymn from the Common Menaion, that is to say, of the Saint. — After the 6th ode, the small litany, the kontakion and ikos of the Saint. Towards the end of the 8th ode instead of “Glory” we say: “We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord”; before the Katavasia the choir saith in the tone of the canon of the Saint: “We praise, we bless, we worship,” etc., and they sing the Heirmos of the 8th ode of the canon of the Saint as Katavasia. After the 8th ode the Priest in the sanctuary proclaimeth: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light in hymns let us magnify,” whereunto the choir loudly singeth: “My soul doth magnify the Lord,” etc., chanting unto the verses: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. After the Katavasia of the 9th ode: “It is truly right.” The small litany and the Exaposteilarion, and that of the day; “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion, or if it be Wednesday or Friday: Stavrotheotokion. And immediately Psalms: 148, 149, and the 150th [Pss. 148, 149, 150] are read (“Praise the Lord from the heavens,” “Sing unto the Lord a new song,” and “Praise God in His Saints”). Upon the conclusion of Psalm 150, the Priest proclaimeth from the sanctuary: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light,” and the choir readeth the Doxology in order: “Glory to God in the highest.”[^118]

After this the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary and before the royal doors saith the litany: “Let us complete our morning prayer.” After the 2nd exclamation of this litany the Priest entereth into the sanctuary, and the choir beginneth to sing the stichera at the aposticha (from the Octoechos) with the usual refrains. (Before the 1st there is no verse; before the 2nd and 3rd: “We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy,” and “Let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us” [Ps. 89:14, 17]). At the end: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.[^119] After this the choir readeth: “It is good to give praise,” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this the troparion of the Saint. “Glory,” and “Now and,” the dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. The Priest after this cometh forth from the sanctuary and before the royal doors chanteth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” The daily dismissal, and that the small one, and the blessing.

§. 52.

Order of Festal Matins Services.

(α) Matins with the Great Doxology. This Matins is celebrated on minor feasts, that is to say, when the feast or the Saint hath stichera on 6 and the Great Doxology. Beginning from the start, up unto the Doxology, it is small,[^120] and only here towards the end of the Praises (*Ainoi*) the Priest vesteth in the epitrachelion and phelonion, and standeth before the Holy Table, awaiting the end of the stichera at the Praises. — When the chanter hath concluded these, then he openeth the royal doors and proclaimeth: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light,” and goeth forth onto the ambo and there with the chanter singeth the Doxology.[^121] After this The Trisagion (thrice). And immediately the troparion of the feast with the Theotokion, after which the Priest entereth unto the Holy Table and there chanteth the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete.”

The great dismissal, and having given the blessing, he shutteth the royal doors. (There are no stichera at the Aposticha.)[^122]

(β) Matins with the Polyeleos, with the Gospel, and with the Great Doxology. It is celebrated on weekdays if there occur a Saint having a polyeleos, or a feast of the Lord, or a feast of the Mother of God. The order of this Matins is as followeth: The Priest, having put on the epitrachelion, cometh before the royal doors, and having made a bow there, saith: “Blessed is our God.” The choir: “Amen.” Then (if there was no Midnight Office): “O Heavenly King.” After this also the rest of the usual beginning. After “Come, let us worship” (thrice), Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20]. And immediately The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. After this the troparia are read: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., after which the litany for the Emperor with the exclamation. Unto the exclamation the choir answereth: “Amen,” and immediately proclaimeth: “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” Unto this the Priest: “Glory to the holy...” etc. The choir: “Amen,” and immediately: “Glory to God in the highest,” etc. (thrice), “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). And there followeth the Six Psalms. (The Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary and reciteth the priestly morning prayers.) After the Six Psalms the Priest cometh forth and the litany of peace is chanted. “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc. (4 times). Festal troparia. Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn of the Saint. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn of the Saint.[^123] After this: “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion, and instead of the 3rd kathisma the choir singeth the Polyeleos (that is to say, verses from the 19th Kathisma: “Praise the name of the Lord,” etc.). After this: The Megalynarion with the verses. After this the small litany is said and the choir singeth the 3rd Sessional Hymn (the polyeleos one), “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. Then the Gradual Hymns of the 4th Tone, the 1st Antiphon: “From my youth,” which are sung to 4 verses, chanting unto the 3rd verse “Glory,” and unto the 4th “Now and.” During the singing of the 3rd and 4th verses, that is to say, at “Glory,” and “Now and,” the Priest vesteth in the phelonion, ascendeth unto the Holy Table, and proclaimeth: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” Then the choir singeth the daily Prokimenon. After the Prokimenon the Priest openeth the royal doors, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” Unto this the choir: “Lord, have mercy,” and immediately the Priest saith the exclamation: “For holy art Thou,” etc. The choir: “Amen.” And again the Priest: “Let every breath praise the Lord,” and censeth the Holy Table and from the royal doors the people. After the choir singeth: “Let every breath praise the Lord” twice, the Priest [saith]: “That we may be accounted worthy to hear the holy Gospel,” etc. Then upon the choir’s response the Priest [saith]: “Peace be to all!” The choir: (“And to thy spirit”). And thereafter: “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and he readeth the holy Gospel, and that standing before the Holy Table. After the Gospel hath been read, immediately Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. (The Priest, after reading the holy Gospel, goeth forth and giveth the holy Gospel to be kissed upon the ambo.) — After Psalm 50: “Glory”: “Through the prayers of the holy Archangel [or: holy Apostle, or: Prophet],” etc. ([Name]), in whose honor the service is celebrated. At “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Most Holy Theotokos,” etc., is sung. At the end the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God,” etc., and the sticheron of the Saint.[^124] After this the Priest, having returned unto the Holy Table, chanteth the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., whereunto the choir [respondeth]: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). And immediately the Priest: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), whereunto the choir again: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). And immediately the Priest [saith] the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions...”[^125] etc. After this exclamation the Priest shutteth the royal doors, divesteth of the phelonion, and the chanter beginneth to sing the canon on 14. In the canon all things are taken in order. Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode the choir proclaimeth the refrain: “We praise, we bless, we worship,” etc., and singeth the Katavasia. After the Katavasia the Priest proclaimeth: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., and the magnification of the feast is sung (that is, the Heirmos of the 9th ode with the refrain) or “More honorable than the Cherubim.”[^126] After the 9th ode: the small litany and the Exaposteilarion. Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*): and that we set 4; “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion. At this the Priest vesteth in the phelonion, openeth the royal doors, and goeth forth onto the ambo, and proclaimeth: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light”; they sing the Great Doxology. The Trisagion. The troparion of the Saint and Theotokion. The Priest, having entered into the sanctuary, saith before the Holy Table the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete.” The great dismissal, the blessing, and he shutteth the royal doors.

§. 53.

Order of Sunday Matins.

It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one, as at daily Matins without any exception. After the Six Psalms, when the choir singeth: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Glory to Thee, O God,” the Priest, standing before the royal doors, saith the litany of peace. After this: “God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us,” etc., which chant is sung in the current tone. The resurrectional (Sunday) troparion (twice); “Glory,” of the Saint; “Now and,” Theotokion from the Octoechos according to the tone of the Saint. After the troparia the choir immediately saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), and “Glory”; the reader continueth: “Now and,” and readeth Kathisma 1. After the kathisma: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and immediately the 1st Sessional Hymn from the Octoechos is sung, first plain, and thereafter unto the refrain: “Arise, O Lord my God,” etc., and at the end with “Glory,” and “Now and.” After the 1st Sessional Hymn the choir: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory”; the reader: “Now and,” and readeth the 2nd Kathisma. After this: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen”; and immediately the 2nd Sessional Hymn with “Glory,” and “Now and,” whereunto its Theotokion is sung. After the 2nd Sessional Hymn, as before, the reader readeth the 3rd Kathisma (this is the 17th: “Blessed are the undefiled”), or [the choir] singeth the Polyeleos.[^127] But instead of the 3rd Sessional Hymn the troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*) are sung: “The angelic host,” with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes.” After this: the small litany. The Hypakoe of the tone. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*). After the Gradual Hymns the Priest, vested in the phelonion, proclaimeth from the Holy Table: “Let us attend! Peace be to all! Wisdom, let us attend!” and the choir singeth the Prokimenon. After the Prokimenon the Priest openeth the royal doors, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” Whereunto the choir answereth: “Lord, have mercy,” and immediately he proclaimeth: “For holy art Thou, O our God,” etc. Again after this exclamation the Priest saith once: “Let every breath praise the Lord,” and the chanter [saith it] twice. After this the Priest beginneth to cense the Holy Table and the people from the royal doors, and proclaimeth: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc.; further: “Peace be to all!” The choir: “And to thy spirit.” Then the Priest: “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” the reading from the holy Gospel according to Mark (or Matthew, or Luke, or John), and he readeth the holy Gospel before the Holy Table. Upon the conclusion of the Gospel the Priest readeth before the Holy Table: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ.” The chanter after this readeth Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], during which the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary onto the ambo and giveth the holy Gospel to be kissed. After Psalm 50 the chanter singeth: “Glory”: “Through the prayers of the holy Apostles,” etc.; “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Most Holy Theotokos,” etc. Then unto “Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great [mercy],” etc., the resurrectional sticheron[^128] is sung in the 6th Tone (“Jesus, having risen from the tomb”). And immediately the Priest saith the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions,” etc. After this the Priest shutteth the royal doors, and the choir singeth the canons; and that: the Resurrectional, Cross-Resurrectional, and Theotokion from the Octoechos.[^129] Only the heirmoi and katavasiai are sung, while the other troparia are read. The troparia of the canons are read on 14 with the proper refrains in order, as was stated. — Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode the chanter proclaimeth aloud: “We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages.” After the 8th ode the Priest proclaimeth: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc.,[^130] and the choir singeth: “My soul doth magnify [the Lord],” refraining: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., with the verses. After the 9th ode the small litany, and immediately the Priest, having come forth before the royal doors, proclaimeth: “Holy is the Lord our God” (thrice). The choir likewise answereth thrice. After this the Exaposteilarion. At the Praises (*Ainoi*) the choir singeth: “Let every breath praise the Lord; praise Him in the highest; to Thee song is due, O God”; and the second verse of Psalm 148 [Ps. 148] with this ending according to the current tone. The other verses of the psalm are read from the 148th and the 149th [Pss. 148, 149]. We set 8 verses from the last verse of Psalm 149 [Ps. 149]: “This glory shall be to all His saints.” And we sing the resurrectional and Anatolian (*vostochny*) stichera with the refrains taken from Psalm 150 [Ps. 150], and with the two resurrectional ones: “Arise, O Lord my God,” etc., and “I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord,” etc. After this: “Glory,” the Gospel sticheron; “Now and,” “Most blessed art thou.” The Priest vesteth in the phelonion, goeth forth before the Holy Table, and maketh a bow before the Holy Table. Upon the conclusion of the singing he openeth the royal doors, goeth forth onto the ambo, maketh a bow, and proclaimeth aloud: “Glory to God, Who hath shown us the light.” The chanter goeth unto the midst of the church, and together with the congregation the Great Doxology is sung. After the Doxology the Priest singeth the Trisagion once, and the chanter twice. Then the resurrectional troparion by both, and that: if it be the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 7th tone: “Today salvation is come unto the world”; but if it be the 2nd, 4th, 6th, and 8th Tone, then: “Having risen from the tomb” (*Molitvoslov*, p. 424). After this the Priest, having bowed, entereth into the holy sanctuary, and taketh the Gospel from the *Tetrapod*, if it was there. After this the Priest, standing before the Holy Table, saith the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. At the end he maketh the great dismissal, commemorating also the Saint in whose honor the service is sung, and giveth the blessing. After the dismissal he shutteth the royal doors.

Note: At Sunday Matins there are taken in order 11 resurrectional Gospels, 11 Gospel stichera, and 11 resurrectional Exaposteilaria, which agree in meaning. Besides this, such are the characteristics of this Sunday Matins, that for the most part it hath no Polyeleos, but hath the resurrectional troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*); at it “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” is read after the Gospel; and at the end it hath “Holy is the Lord our God.”

### 2) Extraordinary Matins Services.

§. 54.

Order of Matins for the Departed.

This Matins is small, without the Gospel and without the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one, only that after the Six Psalms, instead of the litany of peace, the litany for the departed is taken (*Molitvoslov*, p. 328). Instead of: “God is the Lord,” etc., “Alleluia” (4 times), and immediately there are sung in the 8th Tone the troparia: “With profound wisdom,” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Thee, the rampart.” After the troparia, the usual 1st Kathisma, and the 1st Sessional Hymn from the Octoechos. Immediately after the 1st Sessional Hymn: “Blessed are the undefiled” is sung in two stases; and that the first stasis with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou,” etc., and at the end of this stasis the verse: “Unless Thy law had been,” etc. (thrice). After this the small litany for the departed (“Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord”; “Furthermore let us pray for the remission of sins of the servants of God departed in blessed memory,” etc. And the exclamation: “For Thou art the resurrection,” etc. [See *Molitvoslov*, p. 355]), and immediately the second stasis beginneth to be sung: “I am Thine.” After these two stases follow the troparia: “The choir of the Saints,” with the refrain: “Blessed art Thou, O Lord.” After the troparia, the litany for the departed,[^131] and thus only the 2nd Sessional Hymn (“Give rest, O our Savior”). After the 2nd Sessional Hymn, Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canon of the temple with the Heirmos on 6, and the canon from the Triodion on 8. After the 3rd ode of the canon the usual litany, and not for the departed. After the 6th ode the litany for the departed, and the kontakion: “With the Saints.” After the 9th the usual litany, and not for the departed, and the Exaposteilarion: “Ruling over the living and the dead,” etc. All the rest in order. At the Praises (*Ainoi*) 4 stichera from the Triodion; “Glory,” and “Now and,” likewise from the Triodion. The Small Doxology. After this the litany: “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. Stichera at the aposticha for the departed with verses for the departed, and with “Glory,” and “Now and.” Psalm: “It is good to give praise.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion for the departed: “With profound wisdom”; “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Thee, the rampart.” The litany for the departed (*Molitvoslov*, p. 355). The Dismissal.[^132] The blessing, and thus the Priest proclaimeth: “In blessed falling asleep, eternal rest...” etc. The choir: “Memory eternal.” — This Matins is celebrated on All Souls Saturdays.

§. 55.

Order of Matins Services in the Holy Great Fast.

(α) Order of Daily Matins in the Holy Great Fast with the First Hour (excepting Saturdays and Sundays). It beginneth: “Blessed is our God”; but if there was no Midnight Office in church, then: “Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.” “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (thrice).[^133] Psalms: 19 and the 20th [Pss. 19, 20], and all the rest in order. “God is the Lord” is not sung, but instead of that “Alleluia” (thrice) with 4 verses. (On Saturday, Sunday, and on feasts “God is the Lord” is sung.) Instead of the troparia, the 3 Triadica are sung slowly, and that in such a manner that at the 2nd verse “Glory” is taken, and at the 3rd “Now and.” — Let it be noted that at the end of the 1st Triadicon of all the 8 tones the endings of the day are said.[^134] (These endings are given in the *Horologion*, and in the *Molitvoslov*, p. 107.) After the Triadica, Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn. After this: Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. (These ses- sional hymns are the penitential ones of the Octoechos which are found at the end of the Menaion, and the martyr hymn (*Martyrikon*) with the refrain: “Wondrous is God in His Saints.” Besides this, let us also note that in the Great Fast neither after the 1st nor after the 2nd Kathisma is the small litany said, but the choir saith: “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.”) After the 2nd Sessional Hymn the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” and thus readeth Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors and saith the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., with the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions.” After this litany followeth the canon combined with the Biblical Odes (*Canticles of Moses*).[^135] In the Great Fast on weekdays there are 2 canons: of the Octoechos for the day with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion for the Saint on 6. In whichever odes the Triodion (*Triodion-canon / Trepesnets*) occurreth, we omit the canon of the Octoechos with the Heirmos, and sing the canon of the Saint with the Heirmos on 6, and the Triodion on 8. At the end the Katavasia is sung, and that the Heirmos of the Triodion canon.[^136] The Katavasia on weekdays throughout the entire Forty Days is sung thus: On Monday after the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Tuesday after the 2nd, 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Wednesday after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Thursday after the 4th, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Friday after the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. — On Saturday after the 3rd, 6th, 7th, 8th, and 9th ode. After the 3rd ode, the Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion. After the 6th, the kontakion and ikos of the Saint. (If, however, the Saint hath no kontakion in the Menaion, then here the martyr sessional hymn printed at the end of the Lenten Triodion is said.) After the 8th ode, at the exclamation of the Priest: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., “My soul doth magnify [the Lord]” and “More honorable than the Cherubim.” After the 9th ode: “It is truly right” is sung with one bow. The small litany. The Lenten Exaposteilaria according to the 8 tones with the proper endings (*Molitvoslov*, pp. 137 and 106).

The Praises (*Ainoi*) are read.[^137] After this the Priest saith the exclamation: “To Thee glory is due,” etc., and immediately: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.” At this “Glory to God in the highest,” etc., is read. After this the Priest before the royal doors proclaimeth the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Triodion with the usual refrains, and the martyr hymn (*Martyrikon*); “Glory,” and “Now and,” Theotokion.[^138] After this: “It is good to give praise” (twice), etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion: “Standing in the temple of Thy glory,” is read. After this (instead of the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc.): “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Lord, bless,” during which the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors and proclaimeth: “Blessed and glorified is Christ our God,” and saith the prayer: “O Heavenly King.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable,” and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; and the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations with the prayer of St. Ephrem. — And immediately the First Hour beginneth from “Come, let us worship,” whereof mention will be made later.

(β) Order of Festal Matins in the Great Fast, if a feast fall upon weekdays (excepting Saturdays and Sundays). — This Matins is great up unto the Doxology (which is small), and after the Doxology it is small. It beginneth from “Blessed is our God,” and is celebrated in the usual manner. After the litany of peace: “God is the Lord” (and not “Alleluia”). The troparia of the feast. Kathisma 1. The 1st Sessional Hymn. Kathisma 2. The 2nd Sessional Hymn. Instead of the 3rd Kathisma: the Polyeleos and Megalynarion. The 3rd Sessional Hymn. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*: “From my youth”). Festal Prokimenon. Festal Gospel. (“Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” is omitted. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]) with the stichera of the feast. After which the Priest proclaimeth the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc., with the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions.” After the litany the canon of the feast is combined with the Triodion. The Heirmos of the feast, and the Katavasia of the order. At “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” the magnification of the feast. Upon the conclusion of the canon, the Exaposteilarion of the feast, and immediately the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) of the feast. Before the Doxology the Priest proclaimeth: “To Thee glory is due,” etc.; after this he saith: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light,” and the chanter readeth the Doxology. After this the Priest in the epitrachelion cometh forth before the royal doors and chanteth aloud the litany: “Let us complete our morning [prayer],” etc. Stichera at the apos- ticha from the Lenten Triodion. After this: “It is good to give praise” (twice), etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion of the feast. During this the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary and saith the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” After this litany 3 prostrations. And immediately the First Hour beginneth from “Come, let us worship” (thrice). After this Psalms: 5, 89, and the 100th [Pss. 5, 89, 100]. “Glory,” and “Now and,” “Alleluia” (thrice), “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Kathisma of the day. “Glory”: Troparion of the feast; “Now and”: “What shall we call thee?” “Order my steps,” further: “Let my mouth be filled,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), during which the Priest cometh forth and before the royal doors proclaimeth aloud the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), etc. “More honorable,” etc. “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “God be merciful unto us,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations. After this again the Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). And the Priest saith the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light,” and after this the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” etc. The smallest dismissal from “Glory to Thee, O Christ God,” etc.

(γ) Matins with the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete. — This Matins is celebrated on Wednesday evening of the 5th week of the Holy Great Fast. It is a small Matins without the Polyeleos and without the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King,” etc., in the usual manner. After the litany of peace, instead of “God is the Lord”: “Alleluia” (thrice). The Triadica from the Lenten Triodion. Only one, and that the 8th Kathisma, and only one Sessional Hymn from the Triodion. Upon the conclusion of this, Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and after this the Priest before the royal doors saith the litany: “Save, O God,” etc., and the exclamation: “Through the mercy and compassions,” etc. (In the midst of this litany “Lord, have mercy” [thrice] is omitted.) After this the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete beginneth, and that in such a manner that the chanter beginneth to sing the Heirmos: “A helper and a protector,” etc.; upon the conclusion of this the choir singeth the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me,” and the Priest beginneth to read the troparia: “Whence shall I begin to weep,” etc., the people making a bow at each. When, however, the canon of the Venerable Mary cometh, he saith unto the troparia the refrain: “Venerable Mary, pray unto God for us.” Unto the troparia of the Venerable Andrew: “Venerable Father Andrew, pray unto God for us.” At the 3rd ode the Katavasia is sung, and that the Heirmos of the same ode: “Establish, O Lord”; and the Sessional Hymn is read with the small litany. At the 6th ode the Katavasia: the same Heirmos of the Great Canon. After the 6th ode the small litany; the kontakion: “My soul,” is sung, and the ikos is read; further is read: “In Thy kingdom when Thou comest.” Before the Katavasia of the 8th ode is sung: “We praise, we bless, we worship,” etc.; upon the conclusion of the Katavasia: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., and the chanter singeth: “My soul doth magnify [the Lord],” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. After this followeth the 9th ode twice, as Heirmos and as Katavasia.[^139] Upon the conclusion of this there is the small litany, and the chanter readeth the Triadicon Exaposteilarion of the tone, and immediately the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*). After this the Priest proclaimeth: “To Thee glory is due,” etc. (*Molitvoslov*, p. 142), whereunto the choir [saith]: “Amen,” and further the Priest saith: “Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light.” At this the chanter readeth the Doxology, during which the Priest cometh forth in the epitrachelion, and upon its conclusion chanteth the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Lenten Triodion. — After this: “It is good to give praise” (twice). The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The troparion: “Standing in the temple.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), “Lord, bless.” The Priest cometh forth before the royal doors, proclaimeth: “Blessed is He Who is,” etc., and readeth the prayer: “O Heavenly King.” After this: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and.” And the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations. After the prostrations the First Hour beginneth, which is celebrated in order without a kathisma. After the Trisagion we say the kontakion: “My soul.”

§. 56.

Order of Matins with the Akathist.

This Matins is celebrated on the Saturday of the 5th week of the Holy Great Fast. It is Matins with the Great Doxology. It is celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one. After the litany of peace: “God is the Lord,” etc., is sung, for in the Great Fast on Saturday and on Sunday it is always taken. After “God is the Lord” the troparion: “The command secretly given” (twice); “Glory,” and “Now and,” the same. After this followeth Kathisma 1 (the 16th), and the small litany, after which the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader.” And the Priest, vested in the phelonion, openeth the royal doors and before the Holy Table singeth from the Akathist 3 kontakia, ending them with “Alleluia,” and 3 ikoi, ending them with: “Rejoice, Bride unwedded,” alternately. The chanter repetitively singeth unto a kontakion: “Alleluia,” and unto an ikos: “Rejoice, Bride unwedded.” (It is necessary to note that during this singing we do not sit, but stand.) Upon the conclusion of the 3rd ikos followeth the 2nd Kathisma (the 17th): “Blessed are the undefiled,” and immediately the small litany. After this the choir singeth the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” while the Priest, standing before the Holy Table, saith the other 3 kontakia and ikoi. The choir answereth as was stated above. Upon the conclusion of this: Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the canons: of the temple with the Heirmos on 6, and the Theotokion on 6. Katavasia: “I will open my mouth.” (If, however, the temple be dedicated to the Mother of God, then the Theotokion canon is sung on 12.) After the 3rd ode the small litany and kontakion, and again the Priest singeth another 3 kontakia and 3 ikoi. At the 6th ode the canon of the temple ceaseth, and the canon of the Theotokos is sung with the Heirmos on 6, and in addition also the Triodion from the Triodion. Katavasia from the Triodion. After the 6th ode the small litany and the kontakion: “To thee, the champion leader,” and immediately the Priest readeth the remaining 3 kontakia and ikoi from the Akathist; at the end the choir again singeth: “To thee, the champion leader.” After the 8th ode: “More honorable than the Cherubim” is sung. Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) from the Triodion 4. The Great Doxology. The Trisagion (thrice). The troparion of the Theotokos once. 2 litanies. The great dismissal.

§. 57.

Order of Matins Services in Passion Week.

(α) Order of Matins on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday of Passion Week. These Matins services are small with the Gospel, but without the Great Doxology. They are celebrated thus: The beginning is the usual one. After the Six Psalms the litany of peace and “Alleluia” (thrice) in the 8th Tone. Troparion: “Behold, the Bridegroom” (thrice) aloud. Then the usual kathismata and sessional hymns from the Triodion. Upon the conclusion of these the Priest, having put on the phelonion, openeth the royal doors and saith: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., as at other times, and readeth the holy Gospel. After the Gospel the chanter readeth Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and the Priest chanteth aloud the litany: “Save, O God, Thy people,” etc. After this litany, the Triodion canon from the Triodion (and on Tuesday, the Two-Ode Canon [*Dvoopesnets*]). After the 8th ode “More honorable than the Cherubim” is not sung, but the Heirmos. (From Lazarus Saturday until New Sunday “More honorable than the Cherubim” is not sung, but instead of that the Heirmos of the 9th ode is taken.) After the 9th ode the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors for a bow. After the 9th ode the Exaposteilarion: “Thy bridal chamber” (thrice). The rest in order. After the aposticha: “It is good to give praise,” and the rest in the usual manner with prostrations. And the First Hour beginneth. Dismissal: “The Lord, coming unto His voluntary passion.”

(β) Order of Matins on Thursday of Passion Week. This Matins is small without the Great Doxology. The beginning is the usual one. The litany of peace, after this “Alleluia” (thrice) in Tone 8. Troparion: “When the glorious disciples” (thrice), and after this the Priest, vested in the phelonion, immediately openeth the royal doors, proclaiming: “That we may be accounted worthy.” “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and immediately readeth the Gospel. (Kathismata and Sessional Hymns there are none.) After the Gospel: Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the canon from the Triodion is sung. (The litany: “Save, O God,” etc., is not said.) The rest in the usual manner, as on Monday. At the end of Matins: “It is good to give praise.” Troparion: “When the glorious disciples,” etc. Litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc. After the litany immediately the First Hour from “Come, let us worship,” wherein we say the troparion: “When the glorious disciples.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: Troparion of the prophecy from the Triodion: “He Who was hanged for the sake of mankind,” etc. After this: the Prokimenon, the Old Testament reading (*Paroemia*), and immediately the Prokimenon. And so: “Order my steps.” “Let my mouth be filled.” After the Trisagion: the kontakion of the Triodion: “Having taken the bread,” etc. And the dismissal.

Note: Since the prostrations already cease on Wednesday at the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, therefore this Matins is without prostrations.

(γ) Order of Matins on Holy Great Friday.[^140] This Matins is small. It beginneth from: “Blessed is our God.” After this: “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (thrice), etc., in the usual manner. “Glory to the holy,” etc. The Six Psalms. The litany of peace. Instead of “God is the Lord,” there is sung: “Alleluia” (thrice). Troparion: “When the glorious disciples,” and that thrice. During this the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary, and vesteth in the phelonion, and having opened the royal doors, censeth the Holy Table, and upon the conclusion of the troparion the chanter singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (twice), “To Thee, O Lord,” “Amen,” whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., “Peace be to all,” etc., “Wisdom, let us attend, let us hear the holy Gospel,” etc., and readeth the holy Gospel at the royal doors. The choir before the Gospel saith: “Glory to Thy passions, O Lord,” and after the Gospel: “Glory to Thy long-suffering, O Lord.” After the Gospel the Priest shutteth the royal doors, and the chanter singeth 3 antiphons from the Triodion. After the 3rd antiphon, the Sessional Hymn; “Glory”: its kontakion; “Now and”: the very same. After this the Priest openeth again the royal doors, censeth, and again saith: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the rest—as before. After the Gospel he shutteth the royal doors. As after the 1st, so also after the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and after the 5th Gospel (there are therefore then 12 Gospels) there are three antiphons and one Sessional Hymn. After the 6th Gospel there are the Beati- tudes (*Blazhenny*), which are read, and after which the Priest from the sanctuary proclaimeth: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and the Prokimenon is sung. After the Prokimenon: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., and the 7th Gospel is read. After the 7th Gospel Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], “Have mercy on me, O God,” is read, after which immediately: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the 8th Gospel is read. (Here the censing ceaseth.) After the 8th Gospel: the Three-Ode Canon (*Triodion*). In the Three-Ode Canon before the 9th ode of the canon: “We praise, we bless,” etc., and the Priest proclaimeth: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” etc., whereunto “More honorable than the Cherubim” is not sung, but the Heirmos. After the 9th ode there is a litany, the Exaposteilarion. After the Exaposteilarion: “That we may be accounted worthy,” etc., and the 9th Gospel is read; and after the 9th Gospel, the stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*). After this, the 10th Gospel. After the 10th Gospel immediately the Priest proclaimeth: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light,” and the Small Doxology is read. After this the Priest cometh forth in the epitrachelion before the royal doors and chanteth the litany: “Let us complete.” After the litany: “That we may be accounted worthy,” and the 11th Gospel is read, and the stichera at the aposticha with their refrains. After the aposticha, the 12th Gospel. After the 12th Gospel: “It is good to give praise,” etc. (twice). Further: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Troparion: “Thou hast redeemed us.” After this the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” before the royal doors. The small dismissal. The Priest: “Wisdom”; the choir: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. — (The First Hour is not joined unto this Matins.)

§. 58.

Order of Matins on Holy Great Saturday.

This Matins is great with stases (*statii*). The Priest in a black epitrachelion maketh the usual beginning: “Blessed is our God,” “O Heavenly King,” and the rest in the usual manner. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and immediately the Priest proclaimeth: “Glory to the holy,” etc. (From “Come, let us worship” [thrice] even unto “Glory to the holy,” the psalms, troparia, and litany found there are omitted.) After “Glory to the holy,” there is said: “Glory to God in the highest,” etc. (thrice), and “O Lord, open Thou my lips,” etc. (twice). The Six Psalms. The litany of peace. At “God is the Lord,” the troparion: “The noble Joseph”; “Glory”: “When Thou didst descend”; “Now and”: “Unto the myrrh-bearing women.” After this the small litany, and there is sung: “Blessed are the undefiled” (Kathisma 17) with refrains in 3 stases, and that in such wise that the chanter beginneth to sing the refrains,[^141] while the Priest, vested in the phelonion before the holy tomb, singeth the troparia. The 1st and 2nd stasis is sung in Tone 5, and the 3rd in Tone 3. After the 1st and 2nd stasis there is the small litany. After the 3rd stasis there are the troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*): “The angelic host.” The small litany and the Sessional Hymn. After this Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], and immediately the canon: “Unto Him Who of old drowned,” wherein before the 9th ode at the exclamation of the Priest: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” there is sung: “Weep not for Me, O Mother.” After the 9th ode, instead of the Exaposteilarion, there is sung: “Holy is the Lord our God” (thrice). Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) 4. After this the Priest vesteth in the phelonion and in all the priestly vestments, and from the sanctuary cometh forth before the royal doors onto the ambo, proclaiming: “Glory to God Who hath shown us the light.” Whereunto the Great Doxology is sung. — After the Doxology, the Trisagion. After the Trisagion: “Come, let us bless Joseph.” During which the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary, and with the Gospel maketh the entrance around the Holy Table, candle-bearers going before, and coming before the royal doors, proclaimeth: “Wisdom, let us attend,”[^142] while the choir singeth: “The noble Joseph,” and “He Who holdeth the ends [of the earth].” During this the Priest goeth unto the high place, and there proclaimeth: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and they sing the Prokimenon: “Arise, O Lord,” etc. The reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel. After the Old Testament reading (*Paroemia*), the Prokimenon, and the reading from the Epistle of the holy Apostle to the Corinthians, pericope 133. After the Epistle, the Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 114. Then the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal. (After the dismissal there taketh place the kissing of the Shroud [*Plashchanytsia*], when the chanters sing the stichera in Tone 5: “Come, let us bless Joseph.”)

§. 59.

Order of Matins Services in Bright Week.

(α) Order of Matins on the Sunday of Pascha. This Matins is without the Great Doxology, instead whereof the “Paschal Stichera” are sung. It is celebrated thus: The Priest, vested in all the priestly vestments, having taken the holy cross, openeth the royal doors, and goeth outside of the church, and maketh a circuit of the entire church, candle-bearers going before, while singing: “Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior,” etc. After this, when all have stood before the closed church doors, the Priest, holding the cross in his hands, proclaimeth: “Glory to the holy,” etc. After this he singeth: “Christ is risen” once, and the choir twice. After this the Priest singeth the verses: “Let God arise,” and the rest, and unto each the choir singeth the troparion: “Christ is risen,” in a higher voice, and repeateth the words: “And unto those in the tombs bestowing life”—when he openeth with the holy cross the church doors, and all enter into the church, singing: “And unto those in the tombs bestowing life.” When the Priest hath entered into the holy sanctuary, he there at the Holy Table chanteth the litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord,” and there followeth the Canon of St. John of Damascus on 16 (in our cathedral church on 6). After all the odes there are small litanies, when the Priest censeth the Holy Table, the icons, and maketh a circuit with the censer among the people, and censeth the people also, greeting each person: “Christ is risen.” The exclamation is said by the Priest within the sanctuary. At the 9th ode the refrain: “The angel cried.” Exaposteilarion: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh,” etc. (thrice). After the canon they sing: “Let every breath” in Tone 1, and there follow 4 stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*), and the Paschal Stichera: “Today a sacred Pascha,” with their refrains. When the stichera begin to be sung, the Priest cometh forth with the Gospel and with the cross onto the ambo, and the people kiss the cross, the Gospel, the *Artos*, etc. These stichera conclude with: “Christ is risen” (thrice). After this the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” “Let us complete,” and so the Paschal dismissal with the cross.[^143] Before the blessing the Priest, having turned unto the people, exalteth the cross, saying thrice: “Christ is risen,” whereunto the people answer: “Truly He is risen,” and only after this doth he give the blessing with the cross.

(β) Order of Sunday Matins, if on any day in Bright Week there fall a Saint with the Polyeleos. This Matins is great, yet in such wise that instead of the Great Doxology the Paschal Stichera are sung. The Priest beginneth from: “Glory to the holy.” Immediately: “Christ is risen” (thrice) and with the verses: “Let God arise.” “Glory”: Troparion to the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. After this the litany: “In peace let us pray to the Lord.” After the litany (“God is the Lord” is not taken) the Sessional Hymns to the Saint without kathismata. The Polyeleos. The Megalynarion. The 3rd Sessional Hymn to the Saint. Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*): “From my youth,” etc. Prokimenon to the Saint and the rest, as at other times. The Gospel to the Saint; after the Gospel: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ,” etc. (thrice). After this (Psalm 50 [Ps. 50] is not taken) “Glory”: “Through the prayers of the Saint,” etc.; “Now and”: “Through the prayers of the Theotokos,” etc.; unto the refrain: “Have mercy on me, O God,” etc., the sticheron to the Saint. And immediately the Priest saith the litany: “Save, O God,” etc., with the excla- mation. The Canon of Pascha on 8, and to the Saint on 6. The Katavasia is the Heirmos: “The Day of Resurrection,” etc. After the 3rd ode the kontakion: “Though Thou didst descend into the grave,” etc., and the ikos. After the 6th ode the kontakion and ikos to the Saint. At the 9th ode (instead of “More honorable than the Cherubim”) there is sung: “The angel cried.” After the 9th ode the Exaposteilarion: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh.” “Glory”: to the Saint; “Now and”: “Having fallen asleep in the flesh.” At the Praises (*Ainoi*) the Sunday stichera 3, to the Saint 3, and the Paschal Stichera with the refrains (instead of the Doxology), during which there taketh place the kissing of the cross and of the Gospel. “Glory”: to the Saint; “Now and”: “The Day of Resurrection,” and immediately: “Christ is risen” (thrice). “Glory”: Troparion to the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. After this the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” and the Paschal dismissal with the cross, in which the Saint also is commemorated.

Notes: (α) As regards Sunday Matins services from Thomas Sunday unto the Ascension of Christ, concerning these it must be noted that they begin from: “Glory to the holy.” — After this there is sung: “Christ is risen” (thrice). (The verses are omitted.) And immediately: “Glory to God in the highest” (thrice), etc. The usual Six Psalms. After the litany of peace the troparia at “God is the Lord.” The kathismata with the sessional hymns. (On Thomas Sunday the Polyeleos and Megalynarion of the feast.) The troparia after the Evlogitaria (*Neporochny*). The Hypakoe of the tone. The Gradual Hymns (*Stepenny*). The Prokimenon. The Gospel. “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” (thrice), etc., in order. The prescribed canon. The Katavasia of Pascha. At the 9th ode the Priest proclaimeth: “The Theotokos and Mother of Light,” and the chanter singeth: “The angel cried.” Stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*), after which there is the Great Doxology, the Trisagion, etc. 2 litanies, and the Paschal dismissal with the cross.

(β) Concerning daily Matins services, we note that these Matins services begin and end in the same manner as the Sunday ones, only it must be noted that after the 2nd Kathisma and sessional hymn the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors and readeth: “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ” once. And immediately Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. After this the appointed canon (of the day and of the Saint). The Katavasia of the order, only after the 3rd, 6th, 8th, and 9th ode. At the 9th ode there is sung: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” The stichera at the Praises (*Ainoi*) are always from the Pentecostarion (*Flowery Triodion*). The Small Doxology, after which the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors and proclaimeth aloud the litany: “Let us complete.” Stichera at the aposticha from the Pentecostarion. After: “It is good to give praise,” the troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast of Pascha. After this the Priest again cometh forth before the royal doors and chanteth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God.” The dismissal without the cross: “He Who rose from the dead.”

§. 60.

Order of Matins on the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.

This Matins is great festal with the Polyeleos, with the Gospel (after which is also “Having seen the Resurrection of Christ”), and with the Great Doxology. Here it is only needful to note that when the chanters after the Great Doxology sing the Trisagion, the Priest, vested in all the priestly vestments, goeth unto the Holy Table, censeth the precious cross displayed there, and carrieth it around the Holy Table, taking it upon his head, before the royal doors, candle-bearers going before, and standing there, awaiteth the end of the Trisagion. Upon the conclusion thereof he proclaimeth: “Wisdom, let us attend,” whereunto the choir singeth the troparion: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc. After this the Priest goeth unto the appointed table, and placeth upon it the precious cross, and censeth it from four sides, and having bowed unto it, taketh it into his hands, and standeth before the table, facing the east, and proclaimeth the litany: “Have mercy on us, O God,” etc., whereunto the choir singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (12 times) in a lower and higher voice, during which singing the Priest lowereth and exalteth the holy cross, blessing the people therewith. — After this he withdraweth unto the right side, facing north, and continueth the litany: “Again we pray for our most devout Sovereign,” etc., whereunto the choir again singeth 12 times: “Lord, have mercy,” as before, during which the Priest again lowereth and exalteth the precious cross; further he goeth unto the 3rd side, and turning unto the west, continueth the litany: “Again we pray for our God-loving Bishop,” etc., whereunto the choir again singeth: “Lord, have mercy” 12 times, higher and lower, while the Priest during the very singing lowereth and exalteth the holy cross, and maketh a blessing therewith. Finally he goeth unto the 4th side, facing south, and chanteth: “Again we pray for the people here present,” etc., and the choir again singeth: “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and all taketh place as before. After this the Priest, standing before the table, chanteth the exclamation, upon the conclusion of which he placeth the precious cross upon the Tetrapod, and there is sung: “Thy Cross do we adore, O Master,” (thrice) with prostrations. After this the Priest kisseth the holy cross, and withdraweth unto the Holy Table, and the people during this kiss the holy cross, and the stichera of the holy cross are sung: “Come, ye faithful,” etc., until the kissing is completed. After this the Priest, standing at the Holy Table, chanteth the litany: “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal.

Note: From this Matins the Matins on the “Sunday of the Adoration of the Cross” differeth in the following: After the Great Doxology, when the choir singeth the “Trisagion,” the Priest goeth unto the Holy Ta- ble, and having censed the cross there, carrieth it around the Holy Table unto the royal doors, and here proclaimeth: “Wisdom, let us attend,” whereunto the choir immediately singeth: “Save, O Lord, Thy people,” etc. During this singing the Priest placeth the holy cross upon the prepared table, and there is sung: “Thy Cross do we adore, O Master,” (thrice) with prostrations. Upon the conclusion of this singing the Priest, having kissed the holy cross, leaveth the Tetrapod and goeth unto the Holy Table, whereupon the people kiss the cross. During this time the choir singeth: “Come, ye faithful,” etc. Upon the conclusion of this the Priest at the Holy Table saith the litanies: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete,” etc. The great dismissal.

### ARTICLE V.

§. 61.

Concerning the Canonical Hours and the Typika [*Obidnytsia*].

The ordinary Hours are: (α) simple and (β) royal. The simple are celebrated daily, that is, on all feasts and simple days; while the royal are on the Eve of the Nativity of Jesus Christ and of Theophany, likewise also on Great Friday.

Of extraordinary Hours we cite: (α) the Hours in the Holy Great Fast; (β) the Hours in Bright Week.

The Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated either separately, when there is no Holy Liturgy; when, however, there is a Holy Liturgy, it is then combined with the Ninth Hour, and then only certain prayers are taken from it for completion.

### 1) Ordinary Hours.

§. 62.

Order of Simple Hours with the Typika [*Obidnytsia*].

The First Hour. If the First Hour is combined with Matins, then upon the conclusion of Matins the chanter immediately beginneth: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). If, however, it is celebrated separately without Matins, then the Priest in the epitrachelion before the royal doors proclaimeth aloud: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). “Come, let us worship” (thrice). (The Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary.) The chanter beginneth to read Psalms: 5, 89, and 100 [Pss. 5, 89, 100]. “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory,” and “Now and”: Troparion of the Sunday, or of the feast, or of the Saint, if there be a great or middle Saint; if, however, there be a small Saint, then “Glory”: Troparion of the day; “Now and”: “What shall we call thee?” After this: “Order my steps.” “Let my mouth be filled.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the Sunday, or to the Saint, or of the feast. If, however, there be a small Saint, then the kontakion of the day is taken. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). During this the Priest cometh forth and readeth before the royal doors the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “God be bountiful unto us,” etc., and readeth the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light,” etc. After this prayer there immediately beginneth:

The Third Hour,[^144] in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). Psalms: 16, 24, 50 [Pss. 16, 24, 50]. “Glory,” and “Now and.” (The Priest entereth into the sanctuary.) “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Troparion of the Sunday, of the feast, or to the great Saint. “Glory”: Troparion to the small Saint; “Now and”: “O Theotokos, thou art the vine”; and there is read: “Blessed is the Lord God,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the Sunday, of the feast, or to the great Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Kontakion to the small Saint. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). After this the Priest cometh forth and readeth the prayer: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., and “In the name of the Lord bless, Father.” Whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and saith the prayer: “O Master God.” Upon the conclusion of the 3rd Hour there beginneth:

The Sixth Hour, and that in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). (The Priest entereth into the sanctuary.) Psalms: 53, 54, 90 [Pss. 53, 54, 90]. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). “Glory”: Troparion of the temple; “Now and”: “Seeing that we have not”; and there is read: “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us,” etc. The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the temple. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). And the Priest cometh forth and readeth: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and saith the prayer: “O Lord and God of hosts.” Upon the conclusion of the 6th Hour there beginneth:

The Ninth Hour,[^145] and that in such wise that the chanter saith: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). (The Priest entereth into the sanctuary.) Psalms: 83, 84, 85 [Pss. 83, 84, 85].

“Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Kathisma of the day. “Glory”: Troparion, as at the 1st Hour; “Now and”: “He Who for our sake,” etc., and there is read: “Deliver us not up.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion, as at the 1st Hour. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). After this the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary and readeth: “Thou Who at all times,” etc. After the prayer the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and,” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest, if all the Hours are celebrated together, proclaimeth: “Through the prayers,” etc. (if, however, they are not: “God be bountiful unto us,”[^146] etc.), and readeth the prayer: “O Master, Lord Jesus Christ,” etc., and the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary. And he maketh a dismissal only then if he wisheth to celebrate the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] separately and apart.

The Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated then if there will be no Liturgy, and it is celebrated thus: The Priest having given the blessing, the chanter maketh the usual beginning and readeth the Psalms with the refrain: “Bless the Lord, O my soul,[^147] blessed art Thou, O Lord.” “Glory”: “Praise the Lord, O my soul”; “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son” (is sung). The Beatitudes (*Blazhenny*). Stichera at the Beatitudes from the Octoechos according to the 8 tones. After this the Priest, having put on the phelonion, openeth the royal doors and proclaimeth: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend,” and the chanter singeth the Prokimenon of the day. After this followeth: the Epistle (*Apostol*). During the reading of the Epistle the Priest censeth the sanctuary, and after it saith: “Peace be to thee, wisdom, let us attend,” and immediately: “Wisdom, let us attend,” etc., and readeth the holy Gospel. After the Gospel he shutteth the royal doors. After this the choir singeth: “Remember us, O Lord.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father” with the exclamation. The kontakion of the temple, of the day, and to the Saint. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” “In the name of the Lord bless, Father”; whereunto the Priest, having come in the epitrachelion before the royal doors, saith: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and readeth the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this the chanter singeth: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (thrice). “I will bless the Lord.” “It is meet and right... unto “the Mother of our God.” After this: “Wisdom.” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc. The small dismissal.

If, however, the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] is celebrated with the Ninth Hour before the Holy Liturgy, then all things are omitted from the Typika that will be taken at the Liturgy, namely: “Only-begotten Son.” The Prokimenon. The Epistle (*Apostol*). The Gospel. The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Blessed be the name of the Lord.” “I will bless the Lord,” etc., and the remaining parts are joined unto the Ninth Hour for completion.

§. 63.

Order of the Royal Hours.

They are celebrated in the same manner as the ordinary Hours. — Here it is only needful to note that at all the Hours, instead of the usual psalms, other psalms are prescribed. Besides this, after the troparia 3 stichera are sung with “Glory,” and “Now and.” After this the Priest from the sanctuary saith: “Let us attend, peace be to all, wisdom, let us attend.” Whereunto the chanter singeth the Prokimenon. After this there is one reading from the Old and one from the New Testament. After the readings the Priest openeth the royal doors, proclaiming: “Wisdom, let us attend,” etc., and at the royal doors readeth the Gospel. After the Gospel he shutteth the royal doors. The chanter readeth: “Order my steps” at the 1st, “Blessed is the Lord God” at the 3rd, “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us” at the 6th, and “Deliver us not up” at the 9th Hour. All the rest are concluded in order, as at the simple Hours, that is, when “Lord, have mercy” is said 40 times, the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors in the epitrachelion, and there saith all things as at other times. After the prayer of the Ninth Hour there are also taken from the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] certain parts for completion, and that: (α) On the Eve of the Nativity and of Theophany: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” “Glory”: “Praise the Lord, O my soul.” “Now and”: “Only-begotten Son.” After this: “In Thy kingdom.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Remember us.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father.” The kontakion of the feast. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), and “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., when the Priest cometh forth before the royal doors, and there readeth the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (thrice). “I will bless the Lord at all times.” “It is meet and right,” and the dismissal. (If, however, there is a Liturgy, then after the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity,” there is immediately the dismissal. “Blessed be the name of the Lord” [thrice] and “I will bless the Lord at all times” are taken at the end of the Liturgy.)

(β) On Great Friday, however, from the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] there is taken: “Bless the Lord, O my soul.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Praise the Lord, O my soul.” “In Thy kingdom remember us, O Lord.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Remember us, O Lord.” “The heavenly choir.” The Creed (*Viruyu*). “Pardon, remit.” “Our Father.” The kontakion. “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim”; and thus “Through the prayers [of our holy Fathers],” etc. The Priest, standing before the royal doors, immediately saith the prayer: “All-Holy Trinity.” After this: “Blessed be the name of the Lord” (thrice). “I will bless the Lord at all times.” “It is meet and right,” and the dismissal.

### 2) Extraordinary Hours.

§. 64.

The Hours in the Great Fast.

The First Hour is celebrated for the most part with Matins, and beginneth only from: “Come, let us worship” (thrice). (The Priest entereth into the sanctuary.)[^148] The chanter readeth the three appointed Psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Kathisma of the day.[^149] Instead of the troparion there is said: “In the morning hear my voice, O my King and my God.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “What shall we call thee, O full of grace?” “Order my steps”[^150] “Let my mouth be filled.” The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion there is taken: “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us.” “Glory”: “Wisdom.” “Now and”: “All-holy [Theotokos].” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). And the Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter saith: “Lord, have mercy” (thrice), “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord,” etc., and the Priest proclaimeth: “God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and show the light of His countenance upon us, and have mercy on us,” and maketh 3 prostrations. After this the chanter: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times). Whereunto the Priest saith the prayer: “O Christ, the true Light.” Upon the conclusion of which the chanter singeth the kontakion of the Annunciation: “To thee, the champion leader,” etc., and if this Hour is combined with Matins, he maketh the smallest dismissal.

The Third Hour, if it is celebrated together with the 1st Hour, beginneth from: “Come, let us worship” (thrice), when the Priest departeth from the royal doors. If, however, it is celebrated separately, then from: “Blessed is our God.” “O Heavenly King.” “Our Father,” etc. After this, following “Come, let us worship” (thrice), the appointed Psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Kathisma of the day. After the kathisma the troparion of the Fast: “O Lord, Who at the third hour didst send down Thy Most Holy Spirit upon Thine Apostles, take Him not away from us, O Good One, but renew Him in us who pray unto Thee.” “Glory,” and “Now and”: “O Theotokos, thou art the true vine”; and there is read: “Blessed is the Lord God,” etc. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion there is sung the troparion of the Fast: “Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God,” with “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times). And the Priest, having come forth from the sanctuary before the royal doors, saith the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” After this the chanter: “Glory,” and “Now and”: “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” and the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. “Lord, have mercy” (12 times), and the Priest readeth the prayer: “O Master God,” etc., and immediately:

The Sixth Hour from “Come, let us worship” (thrice), when the Priest entereth into the holy sanctuary. The chanter readeth the three psalms. “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Alleluia” (thrice). “Lord, have mercy” (thrice). Kathisma of the day. Troparion of the Fast: “Thou Who on the sixth day and hour didst nail to the Cross the sin which Adam daringly committed in paradise,” etc. “Glory,” and “Now and”: “Seeing that we have not boldness,” etc. “Let Thy compassions quickly go before us” is read. After this: The Trisagion. “Our Father” with the exclamation. Instead of the kontakion the troparion of the Fast: “Thou hast wrought salvation,” with “Glory,” and “Now and.” “Lord, have mercy” (40 times), when the Priest cometh forth from the sanctuary and readeth the prayer: “Thou Who at all times.” “Glory,” and “Now and.” “More honorable than the Cherubim,” etc., “In the name of the Lord bless, Father,” whereunto the Priest proclaimeth: “Through the prayers of our holy Fathers,” etc., and maketh 3 prostrations.
