After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the forefeast. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the forefeast; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast. On the Praises the Psalms [Pss. 148–150] are read. Small Doxology. Stichera on the aposticha of the forefeast. After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the forefeast. The rest as usual;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st Hour: Troparion and kontakion of the forefeast; at the 3rd Hour: Troparion of the forefeast; “Glory”: of the Saint;

after the “Our Father”: Kontakion of the Saint; at the 6th Hour: Troparion of the forefeast and kontakion of the forefeast; at the 9th Hour: All things just as at the 3rd. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

§. 104.

XXXVI.) If Forefeast and Two Saints Fall on Weekdays.

(α) At Vespers the Octoechos is omitted. Stichera to both Saints on “Lord, I have cried” are taken at 6; “Glory”: of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast. There is no entrance. The rest as usual. Stichera on the aposticha of the forefeast; “Glory”: Sticheron of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast. The rest as usual;

(β) At Compline: Kontakion of the forefeast;

(γ) At the Midnight Office: All things are in order;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the forefeast once, and the troparion of the 1st Saint once; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast; the rest as before. The Canon of the forefeast at 6, and to the Saints both canons at 8. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Sessional Hymn to the Saints; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the forefeast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the forefeast. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the forefeast and Exaposteilarion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Exaposteilarion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast. Small Doxology. Stichera on the aposticha of the forefeast; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the forefeast. (“Glory”: of the 1st Saint, if he hath one.) After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the forefeast;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st: Troparion of the forefeast, and kontakion of the same; at the 3rd: Troparion of the forefeast and of the 1st Saint; kontakion of the 1st Saint; at the 6th: Troparion and kontakion of the forefeast; at the 9th: Troparion of the forefeast and of the 2nd Saint; after the “Our Father,” however: Kontakion of the 2nd Saint. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

§. 105.

XXXVII.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast a Small Saint Occur.

(α) At Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the feast 3, of the Saint 3; “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; “Now and”: of the feast.

There is no entrance. Prokimenon of the day. Stichera on the aposticha of the feast 3 with its refrains; “Glory”: of the Saint (if there be one); “Now and”: of the feast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. The rest as usual;

(β) At Compline: Kontakion of the feast. The rest as usual;

(γ) At the Midnight Office: All things in order with the usual troparia;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: Troparion of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the feast, after which “Lord, have mercy” 3 times; “Glory,” and “Now and”: and thus Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 8, and of the Saint at 4. Katavasia after Ode 3, 6, 8, and 9, and that is the heirmos of the canon of the Saint. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint; “Glory”: His Sessional Hymn; “Now and”: Sessional Hymn of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the feast. At Ode 9 we sing “More honorable than the Cherubim.” After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. The “Praise the Lord” Psalms [Pss. 148–150] are read. Small Doxology. After the ektenia “Let us complete”: Stichera on the aposticha of the feast with their refrains; “Glory”: of the Saint (if there be one); “Now and”: of the feast. “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]. Trisagion. “Our Father” with the ecphonesis. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us.” Small Dismissal;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st: Troparion and kontakion of the feast; at the 3rd: Troparion of the feast and of the Saint; after the “Our Father”: Kontakion of the Saint; at the 6th Hour: Troparion of the feast; after the “Our Father”: Kontakion of the feast; at the 9th: As at the 3rd. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

§. 106.

XXXVIII.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast a Middle Saint Occur.

(α) At Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the Saint at 6; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. There is no entrance. Prokimenon of the day. Ektenia: “Let us complete.” Stichera on the aposticha 3 of the feast; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. The rest as usual;

(β) At Compline: Troparion of the feast. All the rest as usual;

(γ) At the Midnight Office: All things as usual;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast; “Glory”: Troparion of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After the usual kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the feast. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6 (the Canon of the Octoechos is omitted), and of the Saint likewise at 6;

Katavasia as before. After Ode 3: Kontakion and ikos of the feast, and Sessional Hymn of the Saint. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. On the Praises there are six-stichera Saints who have stichera and a Great Doxology; in this case stichera of the feast 3 and of the Saint 3 are taken; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast; after the Great Doxology, Trisagion. But there are six-stichera Saints who do not have stichera on the Praises, and then the “Praise the Lord” Psalms [Pss. 148–150], etc., are only read; and after this: Small Doxology, etc. Ektenia: “Let us complete.” Stichera on the aposticha of the feast; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast; “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]. Trisagion. “Our Father” with the ecphonesis. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us.” Small Dismissal;

(ε) At the Hours all things are as was said above.

§. 107.

XXXIX.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast a Great Saint (Polyeleos = at 8).

(α) At Great Vespers the Kathisma “Blessed is the man” [Ps. 1] is sung. On “Lord, I have cried” stichera at 8, that is: of the feast 3, and of the Saint 5; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Great Entrance. Prokimenon of the day. Three Readings. Stichera on the aposticha of the Saint 3; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Great Dismissal;

(β) At Compline all things are in order with the Canon of the Theotokos, only there is taken: Kontakion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Kontakion of the feast, etc.;

(γ) At the daily Midnight Office with the 17th Kathisma all things are in order;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: Troparion of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Saint. Instead of the 3rd Kathisma: Polyeleos and Magnification. After this, 3rd Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Graduals [*Stepenny*] of Tone 4, the 1st Antiphon (“From my youth”). Prokimenon and Gospel of the Saint. And immediately Psalm 50 [Ps. 50], sticheron of the Saint. Ektenia: “Save, O God.” The Canon at 14; and that: the Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6, and of the Saint at 8. Katavasia of the day. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion and ikos of the feast and Sessional Hymn of the Saint. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint. At Ode 9: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the feast; “Glory”: of the Saint; and “Now and”: of the feast. Stichera on the Praises of the feast 3, and of the Saint 3; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Great Doxology. Trisagion. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. The rest as usual;

(ε) At the Hours: Troparion of the feast; and “Glory”: of the Saint; the kontakia of the feast and of the Saint we say alternating. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

§. 108.

XL.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast Two Small Saints Occur.

(α) At Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the 1st Saint 3, of the 2nd Saint 3; “Glory”: of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. On the aposticha, stichera of the feast 3; “Glory”: of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us, O God.” Small Dismissal;

(β) At Compline: Kontakion of the feast. The Saints are commemorated only in the Dismissal;

(γ) At the Midnight Office: All things are in order;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast, and the troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. 1st Kathisma, 1st Sessional Hymn; 2nd Kathisma, 2nd Sessional Hymn of the feast. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6, and of the Saints at 8. Katavasia after Ode 3, 6, 8, and 9, and that is the heirmos of the canon of the 2nd Saint. After Ode 3: Kontakion and ikos of both Saints; Sessional Hymn of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Sessional Hymn of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the feast. At Ode 9 is sung: “More honorable than the Cherubim.” After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the feast and Exaposteilarion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Exaposteilarion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. The “Praise the Lord” Psalms [Pss. 148–150], etc., are read. Small Doxology. Ektenias. Stichera on the aposticha of the feast 3 with their refrains; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]. Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us, O God.” Dismissal;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st: Troparion and kontakion of the feast; at the 3rd: Troparion of the feast and “Glory”: Troparion of the 1st Saint; after the “Our Father”: Kontakion of the 1st Saint; at the 6th: Troparion of the feast, kontakion of the feast; at the 9th: Troparion of the feast and “Glory”:

Troparion of the 2nd Saint; after the “Our Father”: Kontakion of the 2nd Saint. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

Note. In this case the office may also be performed in such manner that the stichera of the 2nd Saint sung on “Lord, I have cried” are transferred to Matins, where they are sung on the Praises. Vespers is then celebrated in such manner that on “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the feast are taken at 3, of the 1st Saint 3, etc.

§. 109.

XLI.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast Two Saints Occur, Namely: One Middle and One Small.

In this case the small Saint is transferred to Compline, where his canon is sung with the Canon of the Theotokos; after Ode 9 his stichera are sung. — In this manner this office is performed just as though within the feast there were only one middle Saint.[^167]

§. 110.

XLII.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast Two Saints Occur, Namely: One Polyeleos (Great) and One Small.

In this case likewise the small Saint is transferred to Compline. — The office is performed just as though within the feast there were only one Polyeleos (great) Saint.

Note: It may happen within a feast on a weekday that there are also many Saints: then those Saints either have one combined office, and in this case it is performed as was said above; or each individual Saint hath his own proper office, and then only the office of the 1st Saint is performed, while the others are omitted and are commemorated only in the Dismissals.

§. 111.

XLIII.) If a Saint Having a Litiya Fall on a Weekday and Within a Feast.

(α) At Small Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera 4 and “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Stichera on the aposticha and “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast; after “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast;

(β) At Great Vespers with Litiya: Stichera on “Lord, I have cried” of the feast 3, and of the Saint 5; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Entrance. Three Readings of the Saint. At the Litiya, stichera of the feast (or of the temple) and stichera of the Saint; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Stichera on the aposticha of the Saint; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. At the blessing of the loaves, the troparion of the Saint twice, and “O Theotokos Virgin” once;

(γ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Polyeleos; Polyeleos Sessional Hymn. Gospel of the Saint. Psalm 50 [Ps. 50] with stichera. All the rest as when there is a Polyeleos Saint;

(δ) At the Hours all things are as when there is a Polyeleos Saint.

§. 112.

XLIV.) If It Be a Weekday and Within a Feast There Occur a Temple of a Saint (Male or Female).

Concerning this case it is only necessary to note that here all things are celebrated as when within a feast there is a Saint having a Litiya.

The Apodosis of the temple takes place on the feast itself at Vespers, and that in such manner that the office of the afterfeast is omitted, and on “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the temple 3 and of the Saint of the day 3 are sung; “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion of the same tone. Prokimenon of the day. Stichera on the aposticha of the temple; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the temple; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion. All the rest as usual, and thus the office of the temple is surrendered.

§. 113.

XLV.) If the Apodosis of a Feast and One Saint Fall on a Weekday.

In this case the entire office of the Saint is celebrated a day beforehand (excepting the Entry into the Temple, when the office of the Saint is also celebrated together), and all things are celebrated as on the feast itself, except: The entrance, the Readings at Vespers, the Polyeleos, and the morning Gospel. However:

(α) At Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the feast at 6, sung on the feast itself; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. (On Friday, however: “Glory”: of the feast; “Now and”: Theotokion-Dogmatikon of the incoming tone, except at the Apodosis of the Feast of the Nativity of Christ and Theophany.) There is no entrance. After the prokimenon, “Vouchsafe, O Lord.” Ektenia: “Let us complete.” Stichera on the aposticha of the feast; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the feast once. Ektenia. Small Dismissal;

(β) At Compline: Kontakion of the feast;

(γ) At the Midnight Office: The usual daily troparia; likewise is said also the prayer: “Remember, O Lord, in hope”;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: the same. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the feast. (There is no Polyeleos.) Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The festal Canons; the heirmoi of both canons are sung, if there be two. Katavasia of the day after each ode. After Ode 3 of the canon: Sessional Hymn of the feast. At Ode 9, however, instead of “More honorable than the Cherubim,” the refrain is sung with the heirmos of the feast, and at the Katavasia the heirmos with the refrain. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the feast. On the Praises stichera at 4; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Great Doxology. Trisagion. Troparion of the feast. Great Dismissal;

(ε) At the Hours: Troparion and kontakion of the feast. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things are in order.

§. 114.

XLVI.) If the Apodosis of a Feast and Two Saints Fall on a Weekday.

In this case one Saint is transferred to Compline, and the other Saint is transferred one day beforehand.

§. 115.

XLVII.) If on Weekdays There Occur a Temple of a Saint (Male or Female) with the Forefeast or with the Apodosis of Any Feast.

(α) At Small Vespers: On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the temple 3, of the feast 3; “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. On the aposticha stichera of the temple from Great Vespers. “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. After the Trisagion: Troparion of the temple; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and Dismissal;

(β) At Great Vespers with Litiya: From the Kathisma: “Blessed is the man” [Ps. 1], the 1st Antiphon. On “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the feast 3, of the temple 5. “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. Entrance. Prokimenon of the day and three Readings of the temple. At the Litiya, stichera and “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. On the aposticha, stichera of the feast with the refrains of the feast. “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of the temple twice, and of the feast once. All the rest as usual;

(γ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: Troparion of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the feast and the Polyeleos; after this, the Sessional Hymns of the temple all once each. “Glory”: Polyeleos Sessional Hymn; “Now and”: of the feast. Graduals [*Stepenny*] of Tone 4, the 1st Antiphon. Prokimenon, Gospel, and Sticheron of the temple. The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6, of the temple at 4, and the 2nd Canon of the feast at 4. Katavasia of the day. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion and ikos of the feast, Sessional Hymn of the temple; after Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the temple (and the Synaxarion). At Ode 9 we do not sing “More honorable than the Cherubim,” but the Magnification of the feast. After Ode 9: [Exaposteilarion: ] “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. On the Praises, stichera of the feast 3, and of the temple 3. “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: of the feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion: Troparion of the temple; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. All the rest as usual;

(δ) At the Hours: Troparion of the feast; “Glory”: of the temple; “Now and”: Theotokion of the Hours. After the “Our Father,” the kontakion of the feast and of the temple are said alternating.

§. 116.

XLVIII.) How the Triodion with the Menaion Is Sung from St. Thomas Sunday Throughout the Entire Pentecostarion Season in Any Week, Even unto the Apodosis of Pentecost, Except Saturdays and Sundays, at Vespers and at Matins.

On “Lord, I have cried” during the weeks of the Triodion, the Idiomelon stichera (or Prosomoia) precede: of the feast 3, and of the Saint from the Menaion 3. “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; but if not, “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. On the aposticha, from the Triodion of the day. If two Saints occur, we sing the stichera of the Saints at 6. “Glory”: of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. But if not: “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. Stichera of the feast from the Triodion we sing on the aposticha with the refrains of the feast. “Glory”: of the Saints, if they have one; but if not, “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast; for then the aposticha of the Triodion is not sung. If, however, the Saint hath a Polyeleos, we sing the stichera of the feast 3, and of the Saint 5. “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. If the Menaion is sung at 6, and another great Saint hath a Polyeleos: On “Lord, I have cried” we sing the stichera at 10: of the feast 3, of the daily Saint 3, and of the great Saint 4. “Glory”: of the great Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Entrance and three Readings of the Saint. On the aposticha, stichera and “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. In St. Thomas week, after the Trisagion: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. In the week of the Holy Myrrh-bearers, however: Troparion of the feast: “The noble Joseph”; “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; “Now and”: “Unto the myrrh-bearing women.” The troparion of the feast: “The noble Joseph,” and of the Resurrection: “When Thou didst descend unto death” we alternate when there is a troparion of the Saint. If it be during the week of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman, or of the Blind Man: Troparion of the Resurrection; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: 1st Resurrection Theotokion in the same tone. At Compline: After the Trisagion: Kontakion of the feast. If, however, it be a Saint who hath a Polyeleos, we say first the kontakion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. If in St. Thomas week at Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. If in the week of the Holy Myrrh-bearers: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast: “The noble Joseph” once, and of the Resurrection: “When Thou didst descend” once; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: “Unto the myrrh-bearing women.” If, however, it be in the week of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman, or of the Blind Man: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Resurrection twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: 1st Resurrection Theotokion in the same tone. And the usual kathismata, and Sessional Hymns of the Triodion. “Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ.” Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The Canon of the Triodion of the feast with the heirmos at 8 (heirmos twice, troparia at 6), and of the Saint from the Menaion at 4. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion of the Saint and his ikos and Sessional Hymn; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the feast. If the Saint is sung at 6: The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6 (heirmos twice, troparia at 4), and of the Saint at 6. After Ode 3: Kontakion and ikos of the feast, and Sessional Hymn of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast of the Triodion. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint. If, however, there are two Saints in the Menaion: The Canon of the Triodion of the feast with the heirmos at 6, and two canons to the Saints at 8. Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. If the Saint is sung at 6, and another great Saint hath a Polyeleos: The Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 4 (heirmos twice), and of the daily Saint at 4, and of the great Saint at 6. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion of the daily Saint and his Sessional Hymn; “Glory”: Sessional Hymn of the great Saint; “Now and”: of the feast of the Triodion. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the great Saint and ikos. The kontakion of the feast is omitted here, but we say the kontakion of the feast at the 1st Hour, and at the other Hours we say it alternating with the great Saint. Exaposteilarion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. If, however, the Saint hath stichera on the Praises, we sing 3 stichera of the Triodion of the day: one of the Resurrection, one penitential, and one of the martyrs; and 3 of the Saint from the Menaion. “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. On the aposticha, stichera of the feast of the Triodion with the refrains of the feast; “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; but if not, “Glory,” and “Now and”: of the feast. — If the Saint hath a Polyeleos: On the Praises, 3 stichera of the feast of the Triodion, and 3 of the Saint; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Great Doxology; after the Trisagion: Troparion of the Resurrection; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion in the same tone or of the feast. Ektenia and Dismissal. And the 1st Hour: Troparion of the Resurrection; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion: Kontakion of the feast of the current Sunday, and complete Dismissal. Likewise also at the other Hours we say the kontakion of the feast without alternating. If there be a Polyeleos, at the Hours we say the kontakion of the feast and of the Saint alternating.

At the Liturgy: After the entrance, troparion first of the feast, or of the Resurrection, and of the temple of the Saint (male or female), and of the daily Saint, either two or one. After this, kontakion of the temple of the Saint (male or female); “Glory”: of the daily Saint; “Now and”: of the feast of Pentecost. Prokimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the feast. For the Epistle and the Gospel, we read the daily one first, then of the Saint, even if it be on Saturday.

### ARTICLE III.

§. 117.

XLIX.) How the Entire Divine Worship, and More Especially Matins, Ought to Be Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of a Saint, and the Canon of the Saint at 4?

(α) At Vespers (on Friday) on “Lord, I have cried” stichera of the Saint 3, and of the Octoechos to All Saints 3. (According to ancient typika, stichera of the Saint at 6.) “Glory,” and “Now and”: Dogmatikon of the current tone of the preceding Sunday. (If the Saint have a Doxastikon [*Naslavnyk*], then “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Dogmatikon.) On the aposticha, 3 martyr stichera (the first being on the aposticha, and two being on “Lord, I have cried”). The other stichera with the usual verses of the Horologion [*Chasoslov*]. (The stichera for the departed are omitted, and are sung only during the fast and if there be “Alleluia.”) “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; “Now and”: Theotokion. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone;

(β) At Compline: Troparion of the temple, of Christ or of the Theotokos, and troparion of the day. “Glory”: “With the Saints give rest”; “Now and”: “As the first-fruits.” If, however, it is a temple of a Saint, then the troparion of the day (“O Apostles, Prophets”). “Glory”: “With the Saints”; “Now and”: “As the first-fruits.” The troparion of the temple of the Saint is not said, since in the daily troparion all the Saints are named;

(γ) At Saturday Midnight Office: All things just as in the Horologion [*Chasoslov*];

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. After the kathismata, Sessional Hymns from the Octoechos without the one for the departed. Three Canons: and that from the Menaion of the Saint with the heirmos at 6, of the temple of the Saint at 4, and the martyr canon from the Octoechos at 4. (On Friday at Compline the canon for the departed is sung.) Katavasia after Ode 3, 6, 8, and 9, and that is the heirmos of the canon of the Octoechos. After Ode 3 of the canon, according to the rule, the Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion in the same place. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory”: Exaposteilarion from the Octoechos; “Now and”: Theotokion; all the rest as usual. On the aposticha, stichera of the Octoechos, written on the Praises, 3 martyr stichera with the usual refrains of the Horologion [*Chasoslov*] (“We were filled”)[^168]; “Glory”: of the Saint, if he hath one; “Now and”: Theotokion according to the tone and day. (The Saturday aposticha in the Octoechos is omitted, and is sung only on the 2nd, 3rd, and 4th Saturday of the Fast, or if “Alleluia” occur.) After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2] and after “Our Father”: Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Dismissal Theotokion of Matins, according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st: Troparion and kontakion of the day; at the 3rd: Troparion and kontakion of the Saint; at the 6th: Troparion and kontakion of the temple; at the 9th: Troparion and kontakion of the Saint. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*]: Beatitudes [*Blazhenna*] of the Octoechos at 6. Kontakion of the temple and kontakion of the Saint; “Glory”: “With the Saints give rest”; “Now and”: “As the first-fruits of nature.”

§. 118.

L.) How Matins Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of a Saint, and the Canon of the Saint at 6?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the Doxastikon [*Naslavnyk*]. Sessional Hymns from the Octoechos. “Lord, have mercy” 3 times; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Psalm 50 [Ps. 50]. The Canon at 14; and that: of the Saint from the Menaion with the heirmos at 6, of the temple at 4 [and the martyr canon from the Octoechos at 4]. Katavasia: the heirmos of the last canon; all the rest as before. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint, if he hath one; “Glory”: Exaposteilarion from the Octoechos; but if the Saint hath no proper exaposteilarion, then the Exaposteilarion from the Octoechos; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion from the Octoechos. Stichera on the aposticha: 3 martyr stichera with their daily refrains. “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion. Ektenia. Great Dismissal.

§. 119.

LI.) How It Is Necessary to Celebrate Matins If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of a Saint, and a Saint Having the Canon at 8?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. Sessional Hymns from the Menaion or from the Anthologion [*Trefologion*]. Polyeleos. Graduals [*Stepenny*] of Tone 4, 1st Antiphon: “From my youth,” etc. Canon of the temple at 6, and the Canon of the celebrated Saint at 8. (The Theotokion Canon from the Octoechos is omitted.) Katavasia of the day. On the Praises, Stichera of the Saint at 4. “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion as in the Menaion. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us, O God,” and “Let us complete.” Great Dismissal.

§. 120.

LII.) How Divine Worship Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of a Saint, and Two Saints?

(α) At Vespers (on Friday) on “Lord, I have cried” stichera to both Saints at 6; “Glory”: of the 1st Saint (if he hath one); “Now and”: Theotokion-Dogmatikon of the current tone (of the preceding Sunday). On the aposticha, stichera from the Octoechos, 3 martyr stichera; and that: one being on the aposticha, and two being on “Lord, I have cried.”

The other stichera with the usual verses. “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion from the aposticha. “Glory” (if he hath one): of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion according to the tone; but if the Saint hath no “Glory,” then “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion from the aposticha. After “Now lettest Thou dismiss”: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion of the 2nd Saint;

(β) At Compline: All things just as when there is one Saint on Saturday;

(γ) At Saturday Midnight Office: Likewise;

(δ) At Matins: At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the 1st Saint twice; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the Doxastikon [*Naslavnyk*]. Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos. If it be a temple of a Saint, the canon of the temple is omitted, and the canon of the 1st Saint is sung with the heirmos at 6, of the 2nd Saint at 4, and the martyr canon from the Octoechos at 4. Katavasia: the heirmos of the last canon. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion and ikos of the 2nd Saint, and Sessional Hymn of the 1st Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion in the same place. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the 1st Saint. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion from the Octoechos. Stichera on the aposticha 3, martyr stichera from the Octoechos, written on the Praises, with the usual refrains of the Horologion [*Chasoslov*]. After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Dismissal Theotokion, which is at the end of Matins. All the rest as usual;

(ε) At the Hours: At the 1st: Troparion and kontakion of the day; at the 3rd: Troparion and kontakion of the 1st Saint; at the 6th: Troparion and kontakion of the temple; at the 9th: Troparion and kontakion of the 2nd Saint. — At the Typika [*Obidnytsia*] all things in order.

§. 121.

LIII.) How Matins Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, and the Canon of the Saint at 4?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the Saint. Canon of the temple of Christ or of the Theotokos with the heirmos at 6, of the Saint at 4, martyr canon from the Octoechos at 4. (The canon for the departed is sung on Friday evening at Compline.) Katavasia after Ode 3, 6, 8, and 9: the heirmos of the canon of the Octoechos. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the temple; “Glory”: Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Now and”: Sessional Hymn of the temple. After Ode 6:

Kontakion and ikos of the Saint. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion from the Octoechos. The Praises [Pss. 148–150] are read. Small Doxology. Ektenia: “Let us complete.” Stichera on the aposticha: 3 martyr stichera, which are given on the Praises, with the daily verses. (Aposticha having verses for the departed are not taken, except on All Souls Saturdays [*Zadushni Suboty*].) After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2], Trisagion, and thus the troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Dismissal Theotokion according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint. Ektenia: “Have mercy on us.” Daily Dismissal.

§. 122.

LIV.) How Matins Is Celebrated on Saturday If It Be a Temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, and the Canon of the Saint at 6?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion. All the rest in order. Canon of the temple of Christ or of the Theotokos with the heirmos at 4, of the Saint at 6, from the Octoechos at 4. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion from the Octoechos. The Praises [Pss. 148–150] are read. Doxology as at other times. Stichera on the aposticha: 3 martyr stichera, which are written on the Praises, with the daily verses. (Aposticha having penitential verses are not taken.) All the rest in order.

§. 123.

LV.) How Matins Is Celebrated on Saturday If It Be a Temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, and the Canon of the Saint at 8?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the Saint twice; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion from the greater ones. Sessional Hymns from the Menaion or from the Anthologion [*Trefologion*]. Polyeleos. Graduals [*Stepenny*] of Tone 4, Antiphon 1. Gospel. Canon of the temple at 6, and canon of the Saint at 8. (The Theotokion Canon from the Octoechos is omitted.) After Ode 9 of the canon, Exaposteilarion of the Saint with its Theotokion. On the Praises, stichera of the Saint at 4 or at 6. “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. Great Doxology. Trisagion. Troparion of the Saint; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion. Ektenias. Daily Great Dismissal.

§. 124.

LVI.) How Matins Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, a Temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, and Two Saints Having Full Offices?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the 1st Saint twice; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Resurrection Theotokion according to the tone of the 2nd Saint. If it be a temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, we sing first the canon of the temple with the heirmos at 6, to both Saints at 8. (The canon from the Octoechos is omitted.) After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. On the Praises, stichera of the Saint (if he hath any) at 4; “Glory,” and “Now and”: Theotokion. Small Doxology. Ektenia: “Let us complete.” Stichera on the aposticha: martyr stichera with the daily verses. After “It is good to give praise” [Ps. 91:2]: Troparion of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: Dismissal Theotokion, which is at the end of Matins. All the rest as usual.

§. 125.

LVII.) How Matins Is Celebrated on Saturday If There Be Any Temple Whatever, and Within a Feast the Canon at 4?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Sessional Hymns from the Menaion of the feast. Canon of the feast at 8, of the Saint at 4. Festal Katavasia. (The canon from the Octoechos and the canon of the temple are omitted.) All the rest as usual.

§. 126.

LVIII.) How Matins Is Celebrated on Saturday If There Be Any Temple Whatever, and Within a Feast a Saint at 6?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: Troparion of the feast. Sessional Hymns of the feast. Canon of the feast at 6, of the Saint at 6. (The canon from the Octoechos and the canon of the temple are omitted.) All the rest as usual.

§. 127.

LIX.) How Matins Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, Any Temple Whatever, and Within a Feast a Polyeleos Saint?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast twice; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. (If, however, it is the Apodosis of the feast, then at “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast 3 times). Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6, of the Saint at 6 or at 8. After Ode 3 of the canon: Kontakion and ikos of the feast; “Glory”: Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Now and”: Theotokion. If, however, it is a temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, then “Glory”: Sessional Hymn of the Saint; “Now and”: Sessional Hymn of the feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and ikos of the Saint, if he hath a Polyeleos or Great Doxology. After Ode 9: Exaposteilarion of the feast; “Glory”: of the Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. (If, however, it is an Apodosis, then only the Exaposteilarion of the feast). All the rest as usual.

§. 128.

LX.) How Matins Is Celebrated If It Be a Saturday, Any Temple Whatever, and Within a Feast Two Saints Having Full Offices?

At “God is the Lord” the troparion of the feast and of the 1st Saint; “Glory”: Troparion of the 2nd Saint; “Now and”: of the feast. Sessional Hymns of the feast from the Menaion. Canon of the feast with the heirmos at 6, and to the Saints at 8. The heirmos of the canon of the 2nd Saint. (Concerning the Katavasia, however, it is necessary to note that on weekdays, if there is one Saint, the ordinary Katavasia of the day is taken; if two Saints, then from the canon of the 2nd Saint; and finally: if it is within a feast, then the festal Katavasia is taken. All the rest in order.)[^169]

### APPENDIX.

§. 129.

Concerning the Kathismata.

The Psalter (that is, the collection of 150 Psalms [Pss. 1–150]) is divided into 20 kathismata, and that in such wise that unto the 1st Kathisma belong Psalms from the 1st unto the 8th Psalm [Pss. 1–8]; unto the 2nd Kathisma Psalms 9–16 [Pss. 9–16]; unto the 3rd Kathisma Psalms 17–23 [Pss. 17–23]; unto the 4th Psalms 24–31 [Pss. 24–31]; unto the 5th Psalms 32–36 [Pss. 32–36]; unto the 6th Psalms 37–45 [Pss. 37–45]; unto the 7th Psalms 46–54 [Pss. 46–54]; unto the 8th Psalms 55–63 [Pss. 55–63]; unto the 9th Psalms 64–69 [Pss. 64–69]; unto the 10th Psalms 70–76 [Pss. 70–76]; unto the 11th Psalms 77–84 [Pss. 77–84]; unto the 12th Psalms 85–90 [Pss. 85–90]; unto the 13th Psalms 91–100 [Pss. 91–100]; unto the 14th Psalms 101–104 [Pss. 101–104]; unto the 15th Psalms 105–108 [Pss. 105–108]; unto the 16th Psalms 109–117 [Pss. 109–117]; unto the 17th Psalm 118 [Ps. 118]; unto the 18th Psalms 119–133 [Pss. 119–133]; unto the 19th Psalms 134–142 [Pss. 134–142]; unto the 20th Psalms 143–150 [Pss. 143–150].

These kathismata are read throughout the year thus:

1. From December 20 unto January 14 (that is, unto the Apodosis of Theophany): On Sunday at Matins: Kathismata 2, 3, and 17;[^170] at Vespers there is no kathisma.[^171] On Monday at Matins: 4 and 5; at Vespers: 6. On Tuesday at Matins: 7 and 8; at Vespers: 9. On Wednesday at Matins: 10 and 11; at Vespers: 12. On Thursday at Matins: 13 and 14; at Vespers: 15. On Friday at Matins: 19 and 20; at Vespers: 18. On Saturday at Matins: 16 and 17; at Vespers, however, the 1st Kathisma.

2. From January 14 unto the Sunday of the Prodigal Son: On Sunday at Matins: 2nd and 3rd Kathisma (instead of the 17th, the Polyeleos is taken); at Vespers there is no kathisma. On Monday at Matins: 4, 5, and 6. On Tuesday at Matins: 7, 8, and 9. On Wednesday at Matins: 10, 11, and 12. On Thursday at Matins: 13, 14, and 15. On Friday at Matins: 19 and 20. On Saturday at Matins: 16 and 17. At Vespers the 18th Kathisma is always taken, except on Saturday, where the 1st Kathisma is always taken.[^172]

3. From the Sunday of the Prodigal Son unto the 1st Sunday of the Holy Great Fast, in the same manner as from December 20 unto January 14.[^173]

4. In the Holy Great Fast: In the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 6th week, on Sunday at Matins: Kathismata 2, 3, and 17; at Vespers none. On Monday at Matins: 4, 5, and 6; at the 1st Hour: none; at the 3rd: 7; at the 6th: 8; at the 9th: 9; and at Vespers: 18. On Tuesday at Matins: 10, 11, and 12; at the 1st Hour: 13; at the 3rd: 14; at the 6th: 15; at the 9th: 16; and at Vespers: 18. On Wednesday at Matins: 19, 20, and 1; at the 1st Hour: 2; at the 3rd: 3; at the 6th:

4th; at the 9th: 5th; and at Vespers: 18th. On Thursday at Matins: 6th, 7th, and 8th; at the 1st Hour: 9th; at the 3rd: 10th; at the 6th: 11th; at the 9th: 12th; at Vespers: 18th. On Friday at Matins: 13th, 14th, and 15th; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma; at the 3rd: 19th; at the 6th: 20th; at the 9th again there is no kathisma; and at Vespers: 18th. On Saturday at Matins: 16th and 17th; at the Hours there are no kathismata; and at Vespers: 1st.

5. In the 5th week of the Holy Great Fast, thus: On Sunday at Matins: Kathismata 2, 3, and 17; at Vespers there is no kathisma. On Monday at Matins: 4, 5, and 6; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma. At the Hours: 7, 8, and 9; at Vespers: 10. On Tuesday at Matins: 11, 12, and 13; at the 1st Hour: 14; at the 3rd: 15; at the 6th: 16; at the 9th: 18; at Vespers: 10. On Wednesday at Matins: 20, 1, and 2; at the 1st Hour: 3; at the Hours: 4, 5, and 6; at Vespers: 7. On Thursday at Matins: 8; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma; at the Hours: 9, 10, and 11; at Vespers: 12. On Friday at Matins: 13, 14, and 15; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma. At the Hours: 19 and 20; at the 9th Hour there is no kathisma; at Vespers: 18. On Saturday at Matins: 16 and 17; at Vespers: 1.

6. In Passion Week [Holy Week]. On Sunday, Tuesday, and Wednesday all the kathismata are chanted (that is, in a full day the whole Psalter); on the other days (as also in Bright Week) there is no kathisma; and that in the following manner: On Lazarus Saturday at Vespers: the 1st. On Palm Sunday at Matins: the 2nd and 3rd and the Polyeleos; at Vespers there is no kathisma. On Monday at Matins: the 4th, 5th, and 6th; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma; at the Hours: the 7th and 8th; at the 9th Hour there is no kathisma; at Vespers: the 18th. On Tuesday at Matins: the 9th, 10th, and 11th; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma; at the Hours: the 12th and 13th; at the 9th Hour there is no kathisma; at Vespers: the 18th. On Wednesday at Matins: the 14th, 15th, and 16th; at the 1st Hour there is no kathisma; at the Hours: the 19th and 20th; at the 9th Hour there is no kathisma; at Vespers: Kathisma 18 without prostrations, and thus the Psalter is put aside until Thomas Sunday.

7. From Thomas Sunday[^174] unto September 22 (that is, unto the Apodosis of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross) in the same manner as from December 20 unto January 14.

8. From September 22 unto December 20 in the same manner as from January 15 unto the Sunday of the Prodigal Son.

§. 130.

When Is the Polyeleos Sung?

The Polyeleos is sung: (a) On all feasts of the Lord, of the Theotokos, and of Great (Designated) Saints throughout the entire year, on whatever day they fall, unto which is also appended the refrain of the feast (the Magnification) [*Velichaniye*] with the chosen verses from the Psalms. Likewise the Polyeleos is sung: (b) At Sunday Matins, beginning from the Sunday after the Exaltation of the Precious Cross unto December 20. But from December 20 unto January 14 (unto the Apodosis of the Feast of Theophany), it is not sung, except for a Great Saint having a Polyeleos. After the Apodosis of the Feast of Theophany (from January 14), the Polyeleos is sung on all Sundays, even unto Cheesefare Sunday. It is also necessary to note this further: that on the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, Meatfare Sunday, and Cheesefare Sunday, to the Polyeleos (that is, unto the two Psalms 134 and 135 [Pss. 134 and 135], of the 19th Kathisma), we also chant in addition the 136th, that is, the third Psalm: “By the rivers of Babylon” [Ps. 136] with the “beautiful Alleluia.” On other Sundays, however, the Polyeleos is not sung, but the 17th Kathisma is read, except only for Feasts of the Lord, of the Theotokos, and of Great Saints, if they fall on a Sunday.

§. 131.

On Which Sundays Is the Resurrection Gospel Not Read, and When Is “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” Omitted?

The Resurrection Gospel is not read on Sundays on which a feast of the Lord or of the Theotokos occurreth, for then the festal Gospel is taken.[^175] Likewise “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” is omitted on feasts of the Lord, but on feasts of the Theotokos it is taken. Furthermore, the Resurrection Gospel is not read on Palm Sunday, when also “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” is not said; for “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” is said on Lazarus Saturday. From Pascha Sunday unto the Ascension, “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” occurs also at daily Matins. At the Ascension, the 3rd Resurrection Gospel is read at Matins and “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” is said. — Finally, at the Descent of the Holy Spirit [Pentecost], “Having Beheld the Resurrection of Christ” is not read; but on the Feast of the “Exaltation of the Precious Cross” it is said.

§. 132.

What Is Necessary to Note Concerning the Pillars [*Stolpy*]?

There are 6 pillars.

(a) The 1st Pillar begins on the first Sunday of the Apostles’ Fast [Peter’s Fast].

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 2, from Mark, pericope 70.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 3, from Mark, pericope 71.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 4, from Luke, pericope 112.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 5, from Luke, pericope 113.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 6, from Luke, pericope 114.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 7, from John, pericope 63.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 8, from John, pericope 64.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 9, from John, pericope 65.  

(b) The 2nd Pillar begins after the feast of Elijah.

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 10, from John, pericope 66.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 11, from John, pericope 67.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 1, from Matthew, pericope 116.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 2, from Mark, pericope 70.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 3, from Mark, pericope 71.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 4, from Luke, pericope 112.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 5, from Luke, pericope 113.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 6, from Luke, pericope 114.  

(c) The 3rd Pillar begins after the Exaltation of the Precious Cross.

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 7, from John, pericope 63.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 8, from John, pericope 64.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 9, from John, pericope 65.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 10, from John, pericope 66.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 11, from John, pericope 67.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 1, from Matthew, pericope 116.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 2, from Mark, pericope 70.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 3, from Mark, pericope 71.  

(d) The 4th Pillar begins in the Nativity Fast [Philip's Fast].

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 4, from Luke, pericope 112.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 5, from Luke, pericope 113.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 6, from Luke, pericope 114.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 7, from John, pericope 63.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 8, from John, pericope 64.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 9, from John, pericope 65.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 10, from John, pericope 66.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 11, from John, pericope 67.  

(e) The 5th Pillar begins after the Theophany of the Lord [Baptism of the Lord].

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 1, from Matthew, pericope 116.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 2, from Mark, pericope 70.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 3, from Mark, pericope 71.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 4, from Luke, pericope 112.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 5, from Luke, pericope 113.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 6, from Luke, pericope 114.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 7, from John, pericope 63.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 8, from John, pericope 64.  

(f) The 6th Pillar begins in the Holy Great Fast.

Tone 1. Resurrection Gospel 9, from John, pericope 65.  
Tone 2. Resurrection Gospel 10, from John, pericope 66.  
Tone 3. Resurrection Gospel 11, from John, pericope 67.  
Tone 4. Resurrection Gospel 1, from Matthew, pericope 116.  
Tone 5. Resurrection Gospel 2, from Mark, pericope 70.  
Tone 6. Resurrection Gospel 3, from Mark, pericope 71.  
Tone 7. Resurrection Gospel 4, from Luke, pericope 112.  
Tone 8. Resurrection Gospel 5, from Luke, pericope 113.  

In order to know on which day and month which pillar of the Resurrection Gospels occurreth, we have for this in the Leiturgikon a table arranged according to the boundary keys [*klyuchi hranichni*]. The boundary key is found from the Horologion [*Molitvoslov*] or Psalter, likewise from the tables.[^176] [^177]

§. 133.

Concerning the Ordinary Katavasia.

The Katavasia is sung on feasts of the Lord, of the Theotokos, and of Great Saints, and on Sundays, thus: From September 1 unto September 21 (that is, unto the Apodosis of the Feast of the Exaltation): “Tracing a cross, Moses” [*Krest nachertav Moisey*] and the other odes from the canon of the Feast of the Exaltation, Tone 8. From September 22 unto November 20: “I shall open my mouth” [*Otverzu usta moya*], Tone 4. From November 21 unto December 31: “Christ is born” [*Khristos razhdayetsya*], Tone 1. From January 1 unto the 14th, that is, unto the Apodosis of Theophany: “Thou didst uncover the bottom of the deep” [*Glubiny otkryl yesi dno*], Tone 2. From January 15 unto the Apodosis of the Encounter: “The sun once shone upon the dry deep” [*Sushu gluboroditelnuyu*], Tone 3. From the Apodosis of the Encounter (except the Sundays of the Forty Days [Great Fast] and the Fifty Days [Pentecostarion]) unto August 1: “I shall open my mouth” [*Otverzu usta moya*], Tone 4. From August 1 unto the Feast of the Transfiguration, and on the feast itself: “Tracing a cross, Moses” [*Krest nachertav Moisey*]. In the afterfeast of the Transfiguration and unto the Apodosis: “The choirs of Israel” [*Litsy Izrailtii*]. From August 14 unto the Apodosis of the Dormition: “Adorned with divine glory” [*Preukrashennaya Bozhestvennoyu*]. From the Apodosis of the Dormition unto September 1: “Tracing a cross” [*Krest nachertav*].

In the Forty Days and the Fifty Days, the Katavasia is sung thus: On the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee and on the 2nd, 4th, and 5th Sunday of the Holy Great Fast: “I shall open my mouth.” On the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, Meatfare Sunday, Cheesefare Sunday, the 1st and 3rd Sunday of the Holy Great Fast, furthermore on Palm Sunday and in Passion Week: the katavasia from the Triodion. If, however, a celebrated Saint occur throughout the entire Forty Days on weekdays, then: “I shall open my mouth.” From the Sunday of Holy Pascha unto the Apodosis: “The day of Resurrection” [*Voskreseniya den*]. On Mid-Pentecost, and unto the Apodosis: “Thou didst make firm the sea” [*More ogustil yesi*]. On the 6th Sunday after Pascha and at the Apodosis of Pascha: “Unto God our Savior” [*Spasitelyu Bohu*]. On the very Feast of the Ascension: “Covered by divine protection” [*Bozhestvennym pokroven*]. From the Ascension unto the Apodosis: “Unto God our Savior.” On the 7th Sunday after Pascha, of the Holy Fathers: “Covered by divine protection.” On Pentecost Saturday, from the Triodion. From Pentecost Sunday and unto the Apodosis: “Covered by divine protection.” On the Sunday of All Saints and unto August 1: “I shall open my mouth.”

§. 134.

When Is “My Soul Doth Magnify the Lord” and “More Honorable than the Cherubim” Sung at the 9th Ode of the Canon?

“My soul doth magnify the Lord” is sung on Sundays throughout the whole year, on forefeasts, afterfeasts, and on weekdays. It is not sung, however: on feasts of the Lord and of the Theotokos, and on their apodoses; that is to say: at the Nativity of the Theotokos and at her apodosis; at the Exaltation of the Precious Cross and at its apodosis; at the Entry of the Most Holy Virgin and at its apodosis; at the Nativity of Christ, and on the morrow [Synaxis] and at its apodosis; at the Circumcision; at Theophany, and on the morrow [Synaxis] and at its apodosis; furthermore on the feast days of our holy Fathers, the Great Hierarchs Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, and John Chrysostom; at the Encounter and at its apodosis; at the Annunciation; thereafter from Lazarus Saturday unto Thomas Sunday; on all the Sundays of the Fifty Days [Pentecostarion] (except the Sunday of the Holy Fathers) and at its apodosis; on the Wednesday before the Ascension; at the Ascension and at its apodosis; at the Descent of the Holy Spirit and at its apodosis; at the Transfiguration and at its apodosis; at the Dormition and at its apodosis. Only if a feast or the apodosis of any feast occurreth on a Sunday, then “More Honorable than the Cherubim” is sung.

§. 135.

When Is “It Is Truly Right” Sung at Matins After the Canon, and When Not?

It is sung on weekdays throughout the whole year. It is not sung, however, on Sundays and feast days, on forefeasts and afterfeasts of Saints having a Polyeleos or Great Doxology; on Cheesefare Saturday, on the 1st and 5th Sunday of the Holy Great Fast, and finally: from Lazarus Saturday unto the Sunday of All Saints. (It is not sung also at Great Compline on Monday and Tuesday of Passion Week.)

§. 136.

What Else Is Worthy of Note?

1. “Holy is the Lord our God” [Ps. 98:9] is sung at Sunday Resurrection Matins, except if a feast of the Lord fall on Sunday, for then “Holy is the Lord our God” is not taken. “Holy is the Lord our God” is not taken: at daily Matins, and moreover on the Sunday of Pascha and of Pentecost.[^178]

2. The 11 Matins Resurrection Gospels stichera, from the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee unto the Sunday of All Saints, are sung at Sunday Matins only after the dismissals at “Glory... Now and...”

3. At Great Matins, if there is a feast of the Lord or of the Theotokos, after the Great Doxology only the troparion of the feast is sung; if, however, there is a feast of a Saint, then not only the troparion of the Saint, but at “Glory... Now and...” also the Resurrection Theotokion from the major ones according to the tone of the troparion of the Saint.

4. The dismissals of feasts of the Lord are performed as they are given; nor is it permissible in these to say erroneously thus: “...Christ our true God, through the prayers ... of the holy Ascension, whose feast we celebrate,” etc. In the dismissals of feasts of the Theotokos and of Saints, moreover, it is not permissible to say thus: “...through the prayers of His most pure Mother and of her honorable and glorious Nativity or Dormition,” etc., or: “through the prayers of the holy Forerunner John’s Nativity or Beheading,” etc., because we do not urge the Nativity of the Theotokos, nor her Dormition, nor the Nativity of the holy Forerunner John, nor his Beheading to prayer unto God, but their very persons. — The dismissal must rather be said as was stated above, p. 56.

5. By ecclesiastical rule, every divine service hath the “Usual Beginning” (that is, “Blessed is our God,” “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father,” “Lord, have mercy” 12 times), and each one among us endeth with the “Blessing” (“The blessing of the Lord be upon you”); but if several divine services are joined together, then only at the first is the “Usual Beginning” said, while the others continue only from “Come, let us worship.” “The blessing of the Lord” is said only at the last.

## SECTION II.

§. 137.

Concerning Liturgical Celebrations.

Liturgy (from the Greek word *leitón* [λειτόν] = public, common, and *érgon* [ἔργον] = work) in the broad sense (*sensu lato*) [*in the broad sense*] comprehendeth within itself all divine services, but in the strict sense (*sensu stricto*) [*in the strict sense*] by it is understood solely the unbloody sacrifice of the New Testament. And although the unbloody sacrifice of the New Testament in essence (*in essentia*) [*in essence*] is only one, nevertheless the manner of offering this sacrifice (*modus vel forma offerendi*) [*the manner or form of offering*] both in the East and in the West is not one, and that because both in the East and in the West many Liturgies exist. Among us the Liturgy of the Church of Constantinople [Tsarhorod] is used, and it is threefold, that is to say: (a) The Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom, (b) the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, and (c) the Liturgy of Saint Gregory the Dialogist, or the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.

Of these Liturgies, the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated in the Great Fast on Wednesday and Friday; and in Passion Week on Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday.

The Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is sung: (a) Without Vespers on the Feast of Saint Basil the Great, January 1, thereafter on the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Sunday of the Holy Great Fast. (b) Together with Vespers on the Paramony [Eve] of the Nativity of Christ and of Theophany, if the Paramony be on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday; if, however, the Paramony of both of these feasts fall on Saturday or Sunday, then on the Paramony the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated, and on the very feast day of the Nativity or of Theophany the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great without Vespers. Likewise on Holy and Great Thursday and on Great Saturday, the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is sung together with Vespers.

The Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is sung throughout the entire year, except on the days on which it is commanded to sing the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great or of the Presanctified Gifts.

The Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is not prescribed: (a) In the Great Fast, except on Saturdays and Sundays.[^179] (b) It is forbidden to celebrate the Liturgy on Holy and Great Friday.[^180]

It is necessary also to note this further: that the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated together with Vespers on the Feast of the Annunciation, if this feast fall on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday of the holy Forty Days.

We shall speak of these Liturgies here: (a) In general, (b) according to various occasions.

### CHAPTER I.

§. 138.

Concerning the Liturgies in General.

In the Church of Constantinople [Tsarhorod], as was said, a threefold Liturgy was used, and is even now still used. Each one of these Liturgies consisteth of three parts, namely: (a) Of the Proskomedia, (b) of the Liturgy of the Catechumens, and (c) of the Liturgy of the Faithful. We also in three articles shall speak: (a) Concerning the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom, (b) concerning the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, and (c) concerning the Liturgy of the Presanctified. In each article we shall describe also the aforementioned parts of the holy Liturgies.

#### ARTICLE I.

The Order of the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom.

§. 139.

(a) Concerning the Proskomedia.

The Proskomedia (from the Greek word *proskomizō* [προσκομίζω] = I bring forward, I offer) is the preparatory part of the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great and of the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. — The order of the Proskomedia is as follows:

The priest, wishing to celebrate the Liturgy, upon entering the temple, reciteth the prayer: “I will enter into Thy house, I will worship toward Thy holy temple in Thy fear. Guide me, O Lord, in Thy righteousness; because of mine enemies, make straight my way before Thee” [Ps. 5:8–9]. After this, standing upon the ambo before the Holy Doors, he maketh three bows and formeth the intention before celebrating thus: “I, [N.], an unworthy priest, intend to celebrate the Liturgy and to perform the Mystery of the Body and Blood of our Lord Jesus Christ according to the order of the Holy Eastern Church, unto the glory of Almighty God, and of all the Church Triumphant, and for the benefit of myself and of all the Church Militant, for all who have commended themselves unto my prayers in common and in particular (for the servant of God, [N.], or for the servants of God, [NN.]), and for the welfare of the holy Catholic Church. Moreover, for all the living and the departed, concerning whom Thou, my God, knowest me to be bound to pray and to celebrate the Liturgy unto Thee. May the Almighty and merciful Lord grant unto us joy and peace, amendment of life, time for true repentance, the grace and consolation of the Holy Spirit, and perseverance in good works. Amen.”

Upon the completion of this prayer, the priest, standing there, beginneth the usual prayers before the Proskomedia, saying: “Blessed is our God,” etc. “Amen.” “Heavenly King,” “Trisagion,” “Our Father.” After this the troparia: “Have mercy on us, O Lord,” etc. “Glory”: “Lord, have mercy on us,” etc. “Now and forever”: “Open unto us the doors of thy loving-kindness,” etc.

Upon the completion of this, bowing low, he departeth before the icon of the Savior, and there reciteth the troparion: “We bow down before Thy most pure icon, O Gracious One, asking forgiveness of our transgressions, O Christ God; for of Thine Own good will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver those whom Thou hadst created from the bondage of the enemy. Therefore, thankfully we cry aloud unto Thee: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who camest to save the world; glory be to Thee!” And having bowed before the icon of the Savior, he goeth unto the icon of the Theotokos, and here reciteth the troparion: “Inasmuch as thou art a fountain of mercy, vouchsafe us compassion, O Theotokos; look upon a people that hath sinned; show forth as always thy power, for hoping in thee, we cry aloud unto thee ‘Rejoice!’ as once did Gabriel, the supreme commander of the bodiless hosts.” Upon the completion of this troparion he boweth before the icon, and standeth once again before the Holy Doors, and bowing his head (or with outstretched hands) reciteth the prayer: “O Lord, send down Thy hand from the height of Thy holy dwelling place, and strengthen me for this Thy service set before me, that uncondemned I may stand before Thy dread altar-throne and perform the unbloody sacrifice; for Thine is the power and the glory unto ages of ages. Amen.” After this, before the Holy Doors, he maketh three bows, and entereth into the holy Sanctuary [Altar],[^181] and striking his breast, saith: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner!”

And entering, he again saith: “I will enter into Thy house,” etc. (as above), and having bowed profoundly unto the Holy Mysteries, he kisseth the holy Altar-Table and the Gospel Book.

After this he departeth unto the place where he beginneth to put on the holy vestments. Before putting each of them upon himself, he first kisseth it, and while vesting reciteth the corresponding prayer: thus, upon the Amice [*Napleshnik*]: “I gave my back to scourgings, and my cheeks to smitings, and my face I turned not away from the shame of spittings” [Isa. 50:6]. Upon the Sticharion: “Let my soul rejoice in the Lord, for He hath clothed me with the robe of salvation, and with the tunic of gladness hath He encompassed me; as a bridegroom hath He set a crown upon me, and as a bride hath He adorned me with ornament” [Isa. 61:10]. Upon the Girdle: “Blessed is God Who girdeth me with power, and hath made my way blameless; Who maketh my feet like hind’s feet, and setteth me upon high places” [Ps. 17:33–34; text cited as: Isa. 17:33, 34]. Upon the Epitrachelion: “Blessed is God Who poureth out His gra- -ce upon His priests, like the precious ointment upon the head, running down upon the beard, the beard of Aaron, running down upon the fringe of his raiment” (Ps. 132:2). Upon the right Cuff [*Narukavnytsia*]: “Thy right hand, O Lord, is glorified in strength; Thy right hand, O Lord, hath shattered the enemies, and in the multitude of Thy glory hast Thou crushed the adversaries” (Exod. 15:6–7). Upon the left Cuff: “Thy hands have made me and fashioned me; give me understanding, and I will learn Thy commandments” (Ps. 118:73). Upon the Phelonion: “Thy priests, O Lord, shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice with joy always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen” (Ps. 131:9).

The priest, having vested in the holy vestments, washeth his hands, saying: “I will wash my hands among the innocent, and I will compass Thine altar, O Lord, that I may hear the voice of Thy praise, and tell of all Thy wonders. O Lord, I have loved the beauty of Thy house, and the place where Thy glory dwelleth. Destroy not my soul with the ungodly, nor my life with men of blood, in whose hands are iniquities; their right hand is full of bribes. But as for me, I have walked in mine innocence; redeem me, O Lord, and have mercy on me. My foot hath stood in uprightness; in the churches will I bless Thee, O Lord” (Ps. 25:6–12).

Having washed his hands, he goeth unto the Table of Preparation [Prothesis], upon which the Proskomedia is performed, and upon which a taper burneth even unto the Great Entrance, and striking his breast, he saith: “O God, be merciful unto me a sinner!” After this he arrangeth the holy vessels and saith the kontakion of Holy and Great Friday: “Thou hast redeemed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; having been nailed to the Cross and pierced with the lance, Thou hast gushed forth immortality for mankind. O our Savior, glory be to Thee!” Having laid out the holy vessels upon the Table of Preparation, namely: the Diskos on the right side, and the Chalice covered with a veil on the left side, and the other vessels at the side, he beginneth the Proskomedia with the words: “Blessed is our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.” Then, taking the prosphora into his left hand, and into his right the lance, he maketh the sign of the cross with it thrice over the seal of the prosphora, saying thrice: “In remembrance of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ.” And he cutteth through the right side of the prosphora with the lance near the seal, cutting and saying: “He was led as a sheep to the slaughter.” The left side: “And as a blameless lamb dumb before the shearer, so opened He not His mouth.” The upper side: “In His humiliation His judgment was taken away.” The lower side: “And who shall declare His generation?” Undercutting and taking the “Lamb” from the prosphora, he saith: “For His life is taken away from the earth.” After this, having cut away from it the soft crumb and cutting the “Lamb” crosswise up- -on the reverse side, he saith: “Sacrificed is the Lamb of God, That taketh away the sin of the world, for the life and salvation of the world.” Placing it upon the Diskos and piercing it on the right side, he saith: “And one of the soldiers with a lance pierced His side.” Then he poureth wine into the Chalice, saying: “And immediately there came forth blood.” And having blessed the water, saying: “Bless, O Lord,” he poureth it into the Chalice and saith: “And water; and he that saw it bare record, and his record is true.” (The Chalice standeth on the left side of the Diskos, because the blood and water flowed forth from the left side of the Savior.)[^182] Having thus placed the “Lamb” in the midst of the Diskos, because Christ hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, the priest placeth particles around the “Lamb,” cut out of the prosphora in honor of the Theotokos and of the Saints, and for the intention of the other living and departed members of the Church.[^183]

When he cutteth out the triangular particle in honor of the Most Holy Theotokos, he saith: “In honor and memory of our most blessed Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, through whose intercessions do Thou, O Lord, accept this sacrifice upon Thy most heavenly altar.” Placing the particle of the Theotokos on the right side of the Lamb, he saith: “At Thy right hand stood the Queen, arrayed in a vesture of inwoven gold, adorned in varied colors.”

After this, he cutteth out particles and placeth on the left side of the “Lamb” nine particles (the Nine Ranks), and saith: 1. By the power of the precious and life-giving Cross. 2. Of the honorable, heavenly Bodiless Powers. 3. Of the honorable, glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John, and of all the holy Prophets. 4. Of the holy, glorious, and all-praised leaders of the Apostles, Peter and Paul, and of all the other holy Apostles. 5. Of our Fathers among the Saints, the Hierarchs: Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, John Chrysostom, Athanasius, Cyril, and Nicholas of Myra, and of all holy Hierarchs. 6. Of the holy Apostle, First Martyr, and Archdeacon Stephen, and of the holy Great Martyrs: George, Demetrius, Theodore, and of all holy Martyrs, both men and women. 7.

Of our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers: Anthony, Euthymius, Sabbas, Onuphrius, and of all Venerable Fathers and Mothers. 8. Of the holy Unmercenary Physicians: Cosmas and Damian, Cyrus and John, Panteleimon and Hermolaus, and of all the holy Unmercenaries. 9. Of the holy and righteous Ancestors of God, Joachim and Anna, and of the Saint (whose day it is), and of all the Saints, through whose prayers do Thou visit us, O God.”

The priest after this placeth particles for the living beneath the “Lamb” in the first row, saying: 1. For the Most Holy Universal Pontiff, [N.]. 2. For our most faithful Sovereign, [N.], for all his court, and for his Christ-loving armed forces. 3. For our God-loving Bishop, [N.], for the venerable Priesthood, the Diaconate in Christ, and for all the priestly order. 4. For all the founders and benefactors of this holy temple. 5. On Sunday or on a feast day, and whenever he celebrateth for the parishioners, he saith thus: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, accept this sacrifice for the remission of the sins of all Thine unworthy servants entrusted unto my flock.” 6. On another day, when he wisheth to celebrate for the remission of sins or for any petition of individuals, he saith thus: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, accept this sacrifice for the remission of the sins of Thy servant, [N.], for whom I intend to celebrate the Liturgy, and of the others commended unto my services and prayer.” 7. After this, here he also commemorateth others of the living whom he wisheth, thus: “Remember, O Lord, Thy servants: my parents, superiors, brethren, sisters, and my other kinsmen and friends, and all who have ever done good unto me in spiritual or temporal matters, and even those who hate me and offend me; save and protect them, O Lord, and enlighten and illumine them with Thy grace.” 8. “Remember, O Lord, Thy servants whom I have either grieved, or quarreled with, or judged, and all those whom I have ever harmed in spiritual or temporal goods.” 9. “For the confirmation of the Orthodox, the conversion of heretics and apostates, and for all who stand in need of prayer: save them, O Lord.”[^184]

Then in the second row he placeth particles for the departed, saying: 1. “For the blessed memory and the remission of sins of the Most Holy Universal Pontiff Pius IX, Gregory XVI, and Clement XIV,[^185] and of the other departed Roman Pontiffs.” 2.

“For the blessed memory and the remission of sins of our most faithful Sovereigns, Ferdinand, Leopold, Joseph, Maria Theresa, and of our other departed emperors and empresses.” 3. “For the blessed memory and the remission of sins of our God-loving Bishops, John, Stephen, Basil, Alexis, Andrew, John, and of our other departed Bishops.” 4. “For the blessed memory and the remission of sins of Thy servants, the founders and benefactors of this holy temple.” 5. On an All-Souls Saturday he saith thus: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, accept this sacrifice for the blessed memory and the remission of sins of Thy servants, the Orthodox Christians here lying, and establish them where the light of Thy countenance visiteth.” 6. If, however, he hath a special intention to celebrate for individual departed souls, he saith thus: “O Lord Jesus Christ our God, accept this sacrifice upon Thy most heavenly altar for a sweet-smelling spiritual fragrance, for the remission of sins of Thy departed servant, [N.] (or of Thy servants), and make him (or them) glad where the light of Thy countenance visiteth.” 7. After this, he commemorateth those whom he wisheth, thus: “Remember, O Lord, the souls of my kinsmen, friends, and benefactors,” most especially souls that are in the toll-houses [purgatory]. 8. “For the blessed memory and the remission of sins of all our departed Orthodox fathers and brethren who sleep in the hope of the resurrection and eternal life, and of communion with Thee, O Lord, Lover of mankind.”

At the end, he placeth also a particle for himself between the two rows, saying: “Remember also, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy compassions, mine own unworthiness, and pardon me every transgression, both voluntary and involuntary.”

After this, he blesseth incense and putteth it into the censer, saying: “Incense we offer unto Thee, O Christ our God, for an odor of spiritual fragrance, which do Thou receive upon Thy most heavenly altar, and send down upon us the divine grace and the gift of Thy Most Holy Spirit.” (When, however, the priest celebrateth without a censer, then he omitteth this prayer.) After this, he placeth the Asterisk [Star] upon the Diskos over the “Lamb,” saying: “And the star came and stood over where the young Child was.” Covering the Chalice with the smaller veil [or: Diskos], he saith: “Let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy.” Then the prayer: “The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty; the Lord is clothed with strength, and hath girded Himself; for He hath established the world, which shall not be shaken. Thy throne is prepared of old; Thou art from everlasting. The floods have lifted up, O Lord, the floods have lifted up their voices; the floods will lift up their waves at the voices of many waters. Marvelous are the surges of the sea; marvelous on high is the Lord! Thy testimonies are become very sure;

holiness becometh Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.” After this he placeth the Diskos over the Chalice and covereth it with the larger veil, saying: “Let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy,” and the prayer: “Thy virtue, O Christ, hath covered the heavens, and the earth is full of Thy praise.” At the last, he covereth both with the Aer and saith: “Let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy,” and the prayer: “Shelter us, O God, under the shadow of Thy wings, and drive away from us every enemy and adversary; make our life peaceful, O Lord; have mercy on us and on Thy world, and save our souls, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.” After this, having taken the censer, he censeth the oblation thrice and saith at each censing: “Blessed is our God Who was thus well pleased, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.” And then: “For the precious gifts offered, let us pray to the Lord.” “Lord, have mercy.” After this, the priest with outstretched hands saith the prayer: “O God, our God, Who didst send forth the Heavenly Bread, the food of the whole world, our Lord and God Jesus Christ, as a Savior and Redeemer and Benefactor, blessing and sanctifying us: do Thou Thyself bless † (he blesseth crosswise) this oblation, and receive it upon Thy most heavenly altar. Remember, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind, those who have offered it and those for whom they have offered it; and preserve us uncondemned in the sacred celebration of Thy Divine Mysteries; for hallowed and glorified is Thy most honorable and majestic name, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.”

After this the priest maketh the Dismissal, saying: “Glory be to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory be to Thee!” Glory... Now and forever... Lord, have mercy (thrice). Lord, bless. And if it be Sunday, he saith: “He Who rose from the dead, Christ our true God,” etc. On another day: “Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, by the power of the precious and life-giving Cross, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, and of our Father among the Saints John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, of our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers, and of all the Saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind.” For the departed, however, this Dismissal: “Christ our true God, Who hath dominion over the living and the dead, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, and of our Father among the Saints John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, of our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers, and of all the Saints: establish the soul of His servant departed from us (or the soul of His handmaid departed from us, or the souls of His servants or handmaids departed from us: [N.]), in the tents of the righteous, and give it rest in the bosom of Abraham, and number it among the righteous, and have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and the Lover of mankind. Amen.” If, however, it be a Feast and hath a special Dismissal, then the Dismissal of the Feast is said; but one must always insert the Saint whose temple it is.[^186]

After the Dismissal, the priest departeth unto the holy Altar-Table and, censing the Altar-Table, saith: “In the tomb bodily, in hades with Thy soul as God, in paradise with the thief, and on the throne wast Thou with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ, filling all things, Thyself uncircumscribed.” And having censed the holy Altar-Table round about crosswise, he goeth forth by the northern door onto the ambo, because the Royal Doors are closed, reciting Psalm 50, and censeth first from the ambo the stationary icons [of the iconostasis], and thereafter the entire church and the people, and entereth into the holy Sanctuary [Altar], and laying aside the censer by the holy Altar-Table, beginneth the Divine Liturgy.

§. 140.

(b) The Order of the Liturgy of the Catechumens.
